# AI Image Generation — Specification

**Feature:** AI-Powered Image Generation & Editing  
**Status:** ✅ Production Ready  
**Last Updated:** January 28, 2026  
**Audience:** CEO, PM, Clients, Non-developers

---

## Executive Summary

### What Is It?

AI Image Generation is an automated system that creates professional-quality images for menu items using artificial intelligence. Business owners can generate images for individual items or process entire menus in bulk, eliminating the need for expensive professional photography.

### Why Does It Matter?

- **Cost Savings:** Professional food photography costs $50-200+ per dish
- **Speed:** Generate images in seconds vs. days/weeks with photographers
- **Consistency:** Uniform style across all menu items
- **Accessibility:** Any business can have professional-looking menus

### Who Is It For?

- Restaurant owners who need menu images
- Cafe owners updating seasonal offerings
- Food delivery businesses requiring product photos
- Any business using MenuList who lacks professional photography

---

## Goals & Success Metrics

### Primary Goals

| Goal                    | Metric                        | Target      |
| ----------------------- | ----------------------------- | ----------- |
| Enable image generation | Users can generate images     | ✅ Achieved |
| Support bulk operations | Process 50+ items at once     | ✅ Achieved |
| Maintain quality        | User satisfaction with output | Ongoing     |
| Ensure safety           | Block inappropriate content   | ✅ Achieved |

### Success Indicators

- Users generate images instead of leaving items without photos
- Reduced time from menu creation to completion
- Positive feedback on image quality
- Zero inappropriate content incidents

---

## Target Customers (ICP)

### Primary Users

1. **Small Restaurant Owners**
   - Limited budget for photography
   - Need quick menu updates
   - Value simplicity over advanced features

2. **Multi-Outlet Chains**
   - Need consistent branding across locations
   - Bulk generation for large menus
   - Centralized image management

3. **Food Delivery Businesses**
   - High volume of items needing images
   - Quick turnaround requirements
   - Integration with delivery platforms

### User Personas

**Maria — Small Cafe Owner**

> "I change my menu weekly but can't afford a photographer every time. I need something quick that looks professional enough for my customers."

**Raj — Restaurant Chain Manager**

> "We have 200+ items across 5 locations. I need to generate images in bulk and ensure they all look consistent with our brand."

---

## Scope

### In-Scope (What This Feature Does)

| Capability                  | Description                                                  |
| --------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------ |
| **Single Image Generation** | Generate image for one item at a time with real-time preview |
| **Bulk Image Generation**   | Process multiple items asynchronously with progress tracking |
| **Style Customization**     | Choose photography style, lighting, background, composition  |
| **Reference Image Support** | Use existing image as style reference                        |
| **Image Editing**           | Enhance, change background, add effects to existing images   |
| **Review & Selection**      | Preview generated images before adding to menu               |
| **Progress Tracking**       | Real-time updates during bulk generation                     |

### Out-of-Scope (What This Feature Does NOT Do)

| Excluded                       | Reason                                     |
| ------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------ |
| Video generation               | Different technology, future consideration |
| 3D model creation              | Outside current AI capabilities            |
| Print-ready exports            | Focus on digital menu use                  |
| Custom training on user images | Privacy and complexity concerns            |
| Real-time generation streaming | Technical limitations                      |

---

## User Stories & Flows

### User Story 1: Single Image Generation

**As a** restaurant owner  
**I want to** generate an AI image for a specific menu item  
**So that** I can add a professional photo without hiring a photographer

**Flow:**

1. User opens image upload modal for an item
2. Selects "Generate with AI" tab
3. Configures style preferences (optional)
4. Clicks "Generate Image"
5. Reviews generated image(s)
6. Selects preferred image
7. Clicks "Upload" to add to item

**Acceptance Criteria:**

- ✅ Image generated in under 30 seconds
- ✅ User can preview before committing
- ✅ Multiple style options available
- ✅ Reference image can be provided

### User Story 2: Bulk Image Generation

**As a** restaurant manager with many items  
**I want to** generate images for multiple items at once  
**So that** I can efficiently populate my entire menu

**Flow:**

1. User opens image modal and selects "For Multiple Items"
2. Searches/filters items needing images
3. Selects items for generation
4. Configures generation settings
5. Accepts content policy
6. Starts batch generation
7. Monitors progress in real-time
8. Reviews generated images per item
9. Selects images to keep
10. Uploads selected images

**Acceptance Criteria:**

- ✅ Can select 50+ items at once
- ✅ Real-time progress updates
- ✅ Can cancel mid-process
- ✅ Can retry failed items
- ✅ Images persist until user decision

### User Story 3: Image Editing

**As a** user with existing images  
**I want to** enhance or modify my current menu photos  
**So that** I can improve quality without re-shooting

**Flow:**

1. User clicks edit on existing image
2. Selects editing feature (enhance, background, etc.)
3. Provides instructions if required
4. Generates edited version
5. Compares original vs edited
6. Uploads preferred version

**Acceptance Criteria:**

- ✅ Multiple editing features available
- ✅ Original image preserved
- ✅ Can iterate with multiple edits
- ✅ Business-specific features available

---

## Requirements

### Functional Requirements

| ID  | Requirement                            | Priority | Status |
| --- | -------------------------------------- | -------- | ------ |
| FR1 | Generate images from text descriptions | P0       | ✅     |
| FR2 | Support reference image input          | P0       | ✅     |
| FR3 | Batch processing for multiple items    | P0       | ✅     |
| FR4 | Real-time progress tracking            | P0       | ✅     |
| FR5 | Style customization options            | P1       | ✅     |
| FR6 | Image editing capabilities             | P1       | ✅     |
| FR7 | Content safety filtering               | P0       | ✅     |
| FR8 | Cancel/retry batch jobs                | P1       | ✅     |

### Non-Functional Requirements

| ID   | Requirement              | Target                         | Status |
| ---- | ------------------------ | ------------------------------ | ------ |
| NFR1 | Single generation time   | < 30 seconds                   | ✅     |
| NFR2 | Batch job reliability    | 99% completion                 | ✅     |
| NFR3 | Concurrent batch support | Per project isolation          | ✅     |
| NFR4 | Rate limiting            | 5 req/min single, 3/5min batch | ✅     |

---

## Business-Specific Features

Different business types get tailored image generation options:

| Business Type  | Special Features                                        |
| -------------- | ------------------------------------------------------- |
| **Restaurant** | Food vibrancy enhancement, steam effects, plating views |
| **Cafe**       | Cozy ambiance, coffee art, pastry styling               |
| **Spa**        | Relaxation atmosphere, treatment visualization          |
| **Salon**      | Hair style transformations, before/after                |
| **Tattoo**     | Design preview on skin, placement visualization         |

---

## Content Safety

### What We Block

- Explicit or violent imagery
- Hate symbols or discriminatory content
- Illegal activities or dangerous behavior
- Offensive or vulgar text
- Misleading or deceptive content

### How We Ensure Safety

1. **System Instructions:** AI model receives explicit safety rules
2. **Safety Settings:** Gemini API configured to block harmful content
3. **Prompt Sanitization:** User input cleaned of injection attempts
4. **Content Policy Agreement:** Users must accept terms for batch generation

---

## Risks & Open Questions

### Known Risks

| Risk                               | Mitigation                                  |
| ---------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------- |
| AI generates inappropriate content | Multi-layer safety filtering                |
| Batch jobs fail mid-process        | Graceful failure handling, retry capability |
| High API costs                     | Rate limiting, token tracking               |
| User dissatisfaction with quality  | Multiple generation options, editing tools  |

### Open Questions

1. Should we add cost estimation before generation?
2. Should users be able to save generation presets?
3. Should we support image variations (generate 3, pick best)?

---

## Glossary

| Term                  | Definition                                          |
| --------------------- | --------------------------------------------------- |
| **Single Generation** | Creating one image at a time with immediate preview |
| **Batch Generation**  | Processing multiple items asynchronously            |
| **Reference Image**   | Existing image used as style guide for generation   |
| **Cloud Task**        | Google service for background job processing        |
| **Gemini**            | Google's AI model used for image generation         |
| **Imagen**            | Google's dedicated image generation model           |

---

_Document follows IDE_PROMPTS/6. DOCUMENTATION STRUCTURE PROMPT.md spec template._

## Database Schema

### Firestore Collection: IMAGE_BATCH_PROCESSING_JOBS

**Path:** IMAGE_BATCH_PROCESSING_JOBS/{tenantId}/{storeId}/{jobId}

typescript
interface BatchImageGenerationJobType {
id?: string;
status:
| "queued"
| "processing"
| "completed"
| "failed"
| "cancelled"
| "finished"
| "discarded";
totalImages: number;
generatedCount: number;
generationConfig: {
prompt?: string;
referanceImage?: UserUploadedFileType | null;
stylesCategory?: string;
styles?: string[];
aspectRatio?: string;
environments?: string[];
lighting?: string[];
colors?: string[];
moods?: string[];
compositions?: string[];
backgroundColor?: string;
transparentBg?: boolean;
negativePrompt?: string;
foregroundColor?: string;
selectedImageTypes?: string[];
isMultiMode?: boolean;
agreeToTerms?: boolean;
};
projectId: string;
itemsList: Array<{
id: string;
name: string;
images: UserUploadedFileType[];
}>;
statusHistory: Array<{
status: string;
reason?: string;
createdOn: string | number | Date;
}>;
error?: string;
modifiedOn?: string | number | Date;
createdOn?: string | number | Date;
}

### Status Lifecycle

┌─────────┐ ┌────────────┐ ┌───────────┐ ┌──────────┐
│ QUEUED │ → │ PROCESSING │ → │ COMPLETED │ → │ FINISHED │
└─────────┘ └────────────┘ └───────────┘ └──────────┘
│ │ │
│ │ ▼
│ │ ┌───────────┐
│ │ │ DISCARDED │
│ │ └───────────┘
│ │
▼ ▼
┌───────────┐ ┌────────┐
│ CANCELLED │ │ FAILED │
└───────────┘ └────────┘

## API Contracts

### 1. Single Image Generation

**Endpoint:** POST /api/image-generation

**Request Schema (Zod):**

typescript
const ImageGenerationRequestSchema = z.object({
generationConfig: z.object({
prompt: z.string().optional(),
referanceImage: UserUploadedFileSchema.nullable().optional(),
stylesCategory: z.string().optional(),
styles: z.array(z.string()).optional(),
aspectRatio: z.string().optional(),
environments: z.array(z.string()).optional(),
lighting: z.array(z.string()).optional(),
colors: z.array(z.string()).optional(),
moods: z.array(z.string()).optional(),
compositions: z.array(z.string()).optional(),
backgroundColor: z.string().optional(),
transparentBg: z.boolean().optional(),
negativePrompt: z.string().optional(),
foregroundColor: z.string().optional(),
selectedImageTypes: z.array(z.string()).optional(),
isMultiMode: z.boolean().optional(),
}),
projectId: z.string(),
fileId: z.string().optional(),
businessType: z.string(),
itemDetails: z.object({
id: z.string().optional(),
name: z.string().optional(),
description: z.string().optional(),
attributes: z.array(z.string()).optional(),
category: z.string().optional(),
}),
});

**Response:**

typescript
{
data: Array<{
base64: string; // Base64-encoded image
mimeType: string; // e.g., "image/png"
}>;
message: string;
transaction: {
totalCharge: number;
totalCredits: number;
processingTime: number;
transactionId: string;
}
}

### 2. Batch Trigger

**Endpoint:** POST /api/image-generation/batch-trigger

**Request:**

typescript
{
generationConfig: GenerateImageViaApiPayloadGenerationConfiType;
projectId: string;
businessType: string;
itemsList: Array<{
id: string;
name: string;
description?: string;
attributes?: string[];
category?: string;
}>;
jobId: string;
}

**Response:**

typescript
{
data: {
jobId: string;
}
message: string;
}

### 3. Batch Generation Worker

**Endpoint:** POST /api/image-generation/batch-generation

Called by Google Cloud Tasks for each item. Same request structure as batch trigger but with single itemDetails.

### 4. Image Editing

**Endpoint:** POST /api/image-editing

**Request:**

typescript
{
generationConfig: {
prompt: string;
referanceImage: UserUploadedFileType;
feature?: string;
promptImages?: UserUploadedFileType[];
};
businessType: string;
projectId: string;
fileId: string;
itemDetails: {
id?: string;
name?: string;
description?: string;
attributes?: string[];
category?: string;
};
}

## Implementation Patterns

### Prompt Construction

typescript
// src/app/api/image-generation/prompt.ts

// 1. Sanitize all user inputs to prevent prompt injection
const itemName = sanitizeAIPromptInput(details.name ?? "Subject");

// 2. Build prompt based on reference image presence
if (referanceImage) {
prompt = `Using the provided reference image as the primary visual foundation...`;
} else {
prompt = `A ${styleCategory} image of a ${itemName}...`;
}

// 3. Add configuration options
if (styles.length > 0) {
prompt += `Captured in the style of ${styles.join(" and ")}. `;
}

// 4. Generate multiple prompts for image types if selected
if (config.selectedImageTypes?.length > 0) {
for (const typeName of config.selectedImageTypes) {
generatedPrompts.push(specificPrompt);
}
}

### Cloud Task Integration

typescript
// src/lib/google/cloudTask/index.ts

export async function enqueueImageGenerationTask(data) {
const parent = client.queuePath(PROJECT_ID, QUEUE_LOCATION, QUEUE_ID);

const task = {
httpRequest: {
httpMethod: "POST",
url: IMAGE_GENERATION_WORKER_URL,
headers: {
"project-id": process.env.FIREBASE_PROJECT_ID,
"Content-Type": "application/json",
},
body: Buffer.from(JSON.stringify(data)).toString("base64"),
},
};

const [response] = await client.createTask({ parent, task });
return response.name;
}

### Real-time Listener

typescript
// src/hooks/useImageBatchJobListener.ts

useEffect(() => {
const jobsCollectionRef = getBatchImageJobCollectionRef(session, projectId);

const unsubscribe = onSnapshot(jobsCollectionRef, (querySnapshot) => {
const jobsList = [];
querySnapshot.forEach((doc) => {
jobsList.push({ id: doc.id, ...doc.data() });
});

    if (jobsList.length > 0) {
      setActiveBatchImageJob(jobsList[0]);
    }

});

return () => unsubscribe();
}, [projectId, session]);

### DAL Pattern

typescript
// src/database/imageBatchProcessing/index.tsx

// Collection reference with tenant isolation
export const getBatchImageJobCollectionRef = (session, projectId) => {
const collectionRef = collection(
firebaseClient,
`${COLLECTION}/${session.tId}/${session.sId}`,
);
return query(
collectionRef,
where("projectId", "==", projectId),
where("status", "in", ["queued", "processing", "completed", "failed"]),
);
};

// Update with special field handling
export const updateImageBatchProcessingJob = async (data, projectId) => {
// Handle statusHistory with arrayUnion
if ("statusHistory" in data) {
specialFields.statusHistory = arrayUnion(latestStatusEntry);
}

// Handle generatedCount with increment
if ("generatedCount" in data) {
specialFields.generatedCount = increment(1);
}

await setDoc(docRef, finalUpdateData, { merge: true });
};

## Security Checklist

| Requirement                     | Implementation                   | Status |
| ------------------------------- | -------------------------------- | ------ |
| **Authentication**              | withAuth() middleware            | ✅     |
| **Rate Limiting**               | 5 req/min single, 3/5min batch   | ✅     |
| **Input Validation**            | Zod schemas                      | ✅     |
| **Prompt Injection Prevention** | sanitizeAIPromptInput()          | ✅     |
| **AI Safety Settings**          | Gemini HarmCategory blocks       | ✅     |
| **Content Policy Agreement**    | Required for batch               | ✅     |
| **Tenant Isolation**            | Collection path includes tId/sId | ✅     |
| **Security Logging**            | logger.security() on failures    | ✅     |

### Prompt Injection Prevention

typescript
// Dangerous patterns removed
const dangerousPatterns = [
/ignore\s+(previous|above|all)\s+(instructions?|prompts?)/gi,
/forget\s+(previous|above|all)\s+(instructions?|prompts?)/gi,
/system\s+(prompt|instruction|command)/gi,
/you\s+are\s+(now|a|an)\s+/gi,
/act\s+as\s+(a|an)?\s*/gi,
];

// Special characters removed
sanitized = sanitized.replace(/[<>{}\[\]\\|`~@#$%^&*()+=;:"]/g, "");

### AI Safety Settings

typescript
safetySettings: [
{
category: HarmCategory.HARM_CATEGORY_DANGEROUS_CONTENT,
threshold: HarmBlockThreshold.BLOCK_MEDIUM_AND_ABOVE,
},
{
category: HarmCategory.HARM_CATEGORY_HATE_SPEECH,
threshold: HarmBlockThreshold.BLOCK_MEDIUM_AND_ABOVE,
},
{
category: HarmCategory.HARM_CATEGORY_HARASSMENT,
threshold: HarmBlockThreshold.BLOCK_MEDIUM_AND_ABOVE,
},
{
category: HarmCategory.HARM_CATEGORY_SEXUALLY_EXPLICIT,
threshold: HarmBlockThreshold.BLOCK_MEDIUM_AND_ABOVE,
},

## Testing Guide

### Single Generation Testing

1. **Basic Generation**
   - Select item without image
   - Click "Generate with AI"
   - Verify image generated
   - Verify can upload

2. **With Reference Image**
   - Upload reference image
   - Generate
   - Verify style influence

3. **With Custom Prompt**
   - Add specific instructions
   - Verify output matches

### Batch Generation Testing

1. **Job Creation**
   - Select multiple items
   - Configure settings
   - Accept policy
   - Start generation
   - Verify Firestore job created

2. **Progress Tracking**
   - Monitor real-time updates
   - Verify count increments
   - Verify status transitions

3. **Actions**
   - Cancel mid-job → Verify cancellation
   - Retry failed → Verify retry
   - Upload selected → Verify storage
   - Discard → Verify cleanup

### Image Editing Testing

1. **Each Feature**
   - Test each editing feature
   - Verify prompt generation
   - Verify output quality

2. **Multi-Edit Flow**
   - Apply edit
   - Use edited image as source
   - Apply another edit

## Recommendations & Technical Debt

### Critical Issues (P0) — Must Fix

| Issue                            | Location                      | Impact                                  | Fix                             |
| -------------------------------- | ----------------------------- | --------------------------------------- | ------------------------------- |
| Transaction recording disabled   | route.ts:264                  | Token usage not tracked, billing broken | Uncomment logTransaction() call |
| Debugger statement in production | batch-generation/route.ts:164 | Breaks production execution             | Remove debugger statement       |
| Console.log statements           | Multiple files                | Performance, security                   | Replace with logger utility     |

### High Priority (P1) — Security & Cost

| Improvement               | Current State      | Recommendation                                        | Effort |
| ------------------------- | ------------------ | ----------------------------------------------------- | ------ |
| Cloud Task Authentication | Header-based only  | Add OIDC token verification per Google best practices | Medium |
| Cost Estimation           | Post-generation    | Show estimated cost before batch start                | Low    |
| Batch Size Limit          | No limit           | Add max 50 items to prevent runaway costs             | Low    |
| Rate Limit Bypass         | No Cloud Task auth | Validate task origin with service account             | Medium |

### Medium Priority (P2) — Code Quality

| Improvement           | Recommendation                                                  | Effort                |
| --------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------- |
| Typo: referanceImage  | Rename to referenceImage across codebase                        | Low (breaking change) |
| Duplicate Gemini code | Extract shared generateImage() function for single/batch routes | Medium                |
| No unit tests         | Add tests for prompt generation and sanitization                | High                  |
| Model names hardcoded | Use AI_MODELS from @constant/AI/models.ts                       | Low                   |

## Suggestions & Improvements (From Codebase Cross-Check & Web Research)

### 1. Missing Integrations Found in Codebase

| Component             | Status                    | Purpose                                    | Recommendation                                                    |
| --------------------- | ------------------------- | ------------------------------------------ | ----------------------------------------------------------------- |
| GenerationHistory.tsx | Built, NOT integrated     | Track and reuse generation settings        | Integrate to improve UX - users can regenerate with same settings |
| PromptEnhancer.tsx    | Built, NOT integrated     | Enhance prompts with quality tags          | Integrate with API call for AI-enhanced prompts                   |
| imageQualityGuard.ts  | Built, NOT used in AI gen | Validates image quality (min 400×300px)    | Apply to generated images before upload                           |
| optimizeImage.ts      | Built, NOT used in AI gen | Optimizes images (max 1500px, 70% quality) | Apply to reduce storage costs                                     |

### 2. Google Best Practices (From Official Docs - Jan 2026)

#### Prompt Engineering Improvements

Based on [Google Imagen 3 Prompt Guide](https://docs.cloud.google.com/vertex-ai/generative-ai/docs/image/img-gen-prompt-guide):

| Current Implementation     | Google Recommendation                                                           | Action                           |
| -------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------- |
| Basic prompts              | Use **photography modifiers**: camera proximity, position, lighting, lens types | Enhance prompt.ts with modifiers |
| No quality modifiers       | Add **quality modifiers**: "4K", "HDR", "Studio Photo", "high-quality"          | Add to default prompt suffix     |
| Negative prompts with "no" | Use **plain descriptions** without "no" or "don't"                              | Update sanitizeAIPromptInput()   |
| Fixed aspect ratios        | Support all 5 ratios: 1:1, 4:3, 3:4, 16:9, 9:16                                 | Verify all supported in UI       |

#### Recommended Prompt Template

typescript
// Enhanced prompt structure per Google guidelines
const enhancedPrompt = `  ${subject}, ${context}, ${style}
  Photography: ${cameraProximity}, ${lighting}, ${lensType}
  Quality: 4K, HDR, professional studio photo, high detail
  ${negativePrompt ?`Avoid: ${negativePromptClean}`: ""}`;

### 3. Cost Optimization Opportunities

| Optimization            | Current                | Recommended                                              | Savings Est.    |
| ----------------------- | ---------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------- | --------------- |
| **Context Caching**     | Not used               | Use Gemini explicit context caching for repeated prompts | 20-40%          |
| **Batch Inference**     | Cloud Tasks (per-item) | Use Vertex AI Batch Prediction for 50+ items             | 50% per request |
| **Image Deduplication** | None                   | Hash prompts, cache results for identical requests       | Variable        |
| **Model Selection**     | Always Gemini Flash    | Use Imagen 3 Fast for simple items (cheaper)             | 30%             |

#### Vertex AI Batch Prediction (Alternative to Cloud Tasks)

typescript
// For large batches (50+ items), consider Vertex AI Batch Prediction
// Source: https://cloud.google.com/vertex-ai/generative-ai/docs/multimodal/batch-prediction-gemini

// Benefits:
// - 50% cost reduction vs real-time
// - No Cloud Tasks management
// - Built-in retry and error handling
// - Results in BigQuery or Cloud Storage

### 4. Doctrine Alignment (MenuList Constitution)

| Doctrine Law                                 | Current State               | Alignment                   | Recommendation                                |
| -------------------------------------------- | --------------------------- | --------------------------- | --------------------------------------------- |
| **Law 5: Public Surfaces Demand Perfection** | imageQualityGuard.ts exists | ⚠️ NOT applied to AI images | Apply quality guard to generated images       |
| **Law 6: No Cognitive Load**                 | Multiple style options      | ✅ Good - presets available | Add "Quick Generate" with sensible defaults   |
| **Law 8: Trust > Engagement**                | Quality over quantity       | ✅ Good                     | Consider reducing options, more automation    |
| **Law 2: Silence Is a Feature**              | Shows all generated images  | ⚠️ Consider                 | Auto-select best image, show others on expand |

#### Constitutional Compliance Fix

typescript
// Apply imageQualityGuard to AI-generated images
import { validateImageQuality } from "@lib/imageQualityGuard";

// Before uploading generated image:
const qualityResult = await validateImageQuality(generatedImageBlob);
if (!qualityResult.allowed) {
// Regenerate or reject - don't show low-quality to public
console.warn("Generated image failed quality check:", qualityResult.reason);
}

### 5. Performance Improvements

| Area                  | Current                      | Recommendation                            | Impact                 |
| --------------------- | ---------------------------- | ----------------------------------------- | ---------------------- |
| **Single Generation** | Sequential prompts           | Parallel prompt execution when multi-mode | 2-3x faster            |
| **Batch Job Polling** | Real-time listener always on | Disconnect after 5 min inactivity         | Reduce Firestore reads |
| **Image Upload**      | Full resolution              | Resize to max 2048px before upload        | 50% storage reduction  |
| **Base64 Handling**   | Full image in memory         | Stream to storage directly                | Memory efficiency      |

| **Image Variations**                | Generate 3 variations, user picks best           | Low    | P2       |
| **Partial Batch Retry**             | Retry only failed items, not entire batch        | Medium | P1       |
| **Progress Estimation**             | Show ETA based on batch size                     | Low    | P3       |
| **Favorite Styles**                 | Save user's preferred generation settings        | Medium | P3       |

# AI Image Generation — Marketing & Sales

**Feature:** AI-Powered Image Generation & Editing

## Elevator Pitch (30-Second Hook)

> "MenuList AI turns your menu text into stunning professional photos in seconds. No photographer needed. No expensive equipment. Just type your dish name, and watch AI create appetizing images that make customers hungry. Generate one image or process your entire 200-item menu overnight—all automatically."

---

## Feature Narrative (Business Value)

### The Problem

Every restaurant owner faces the same dilemma:

**Option A: Professional Photography**

- Cost: $50-200 per dish
- Timeline: 2-4 weeks to schedule
- Result: Beautiful photos that need updating every menu change

**Option B: DIY Phone Photos**

- Cost: Free
- Timeline: Immediate
- Result: Inconsistent quality that hurts brand perception

**Option C: No Images**

- Cost: Nothing
- Timeline: N/A
- Result: Lower customer engagement, harder to sell dishes

### The Solution

MenuList AI Image Generation offers **Option D**:

- **Cost:** Included in your subscription
- **Timeline:** 30 seconds per image
- **Result:** Professional-quality images, consistent style, instant updates

### Real Impact

| Scenario                   | Before MenuList                           | With MenuList             |
| -------------------------- | ----------------------------------------- | ------------------------- |
| New menu item              | Wait 2 weeks for photographer OR no image | Generate in 30 seconds    |
| Menu redesign              | $2,000-5,000 for reshoot                  | $0, regenerate instantly  |
| Seasonal update            | Reuse old photos or skip                  | Fresh images every season |
| Multi-location consistency | Each location shoots differently          | Uniform style across all  |

Market narrative (marketing)

So now I can give you a real external judgment, not a partial one.

WHAT I SEE — THIRD-PARTY, NO CONTEXT, NO BIAS
I’ll structure this brutally clearly.

1. WHAT THIS PRODUCT ACTUALLY IS (STRIPPED OF STORY)
   From the outside, this is not “AI image generation”.

It is:

A high-throughput, brand-constrained visual content factory for SMB catalogs.

Key traits:

Asynchronous by default

Bulk-first, not novelty-first

Operational, not creative

Designed for coverage, not perfection

This is closer to infrastructure than creativity tooling.

That already puts it far above 90% of AI image tools, which are:

prompt toys

single-image demos

creativity-first, ops-last

2. WHAT IS EXCELLENT (OBJECTIVELY STRONG)
   A. Bulk Architecture Is Legitimately Production-Grade
   Most competitors collapse here.

You didn’t.

Cloud Tasks per item ✅

Firestore job state machine ✅

Cancel / retry / partial failure handling ✅

Real-time progress via listeners ✅

Tenant isolation baked into paths ✅

This is not MVP-level.
This is “we expect abuse and scale” engineering.

A third-party architect would approve this.

B. Spec ↔ Impl ↔ Marketing Are Surprisingly Aligned
This is rare.

Spec promises bulk → Impl supports it → Marketing sells it

Spec promises safety → Impl enforces it → Marketing avoids hype

Spec promises simplicity → Impl uses presets → Marketing avoids jargon

No obvious lie layer.

That alone removes a huge execution risk.

C. You Avoided the #1 AI Tool Mistake: Streaming UX
You correctly did not do:

streaming partial images

fake “thinking” animations

novelty UX

You chose:

deterministic waits

previews

commit-or-discard

That signals business seriousness, not demo bait.

3. THE REAL PROBLEMS (THIS IS WHERE YOU SHOULD LISTEN)
   Now the uncomfortable truths.

🚩 1. This Is Over-Specified for What Users Actually Want
From a third party POV:

You’ve exposed too many knobs too early.

Styles, lighting, moods, compositions, environments, negatives, presets…

That’s power-user stuff.

But your ICP (as written):

small restaurant owners

cafe owners

SMB managers

They don’t want creative control.
They want “good enough, everywhere, fast.”

Right now the UI reads like:

“You are co-directing a photoshoot.”

They don’t want that job.

🚩 2. The Core Value Is Coverage, Not Quality — But You Sell Quality
This is subtle but important.

The real win here is:

no blank items

consistent visuals

fast rollout

bulk fill

But marketing leads with:

“stunning”

“appetizing”

“professional photography replacement”

That sets the wrong expectation.

If I judge honestly:

80% of value = completeness + speed

20% of value = image beauty

Your messaging flips that.

That creates churn risk.

🚩 3. Cost Control Is Mentioned Too Late
As an outsider, I see:

batch

regenerate

variations

editing

retries

But cost is invisible until after generation.

That’s dangerous.

SMBs are cost-sensitive, even if pricing is bundled.

This needs:

pre-generation estimation

visible “this will generate ~₹X worth of images”

Otherwise:

“I clicked a button and money disappeared.”

That breaks trust.

4. WHAT THIS IS NOT (IMPORTANT CLARITY)
   This is NOT:

Midjourney

Photoshop replacement

Creative exploration tool

Brand storytelling engine

Trying to compete there would kill this product.

This is a catalog completeness engine.

The best comparison is closer to:

“Auto-fill missing product images at scale”

Once you accept that, many decisions simplify.

5. THE SINGLE BIGGEST PRODUCT MISTAKE I SEE
   You haven’t declared a default path strongly enough.

There should be:

one-click “Generate for all missing items”

zero configuration

auto-best-style per business type

auto-pick best image

no modal maze

Advanced controls should be collapsed behind “Customize”.

Right now, power is front-loaded.

That slows adoption.

6. IF THIS WERE A SEPARATE STARTUP — MY VERDICT
   Technical Quality: 8.5 / 10
   Solid, scalable, production-safe.

Product Clarity: 6.5 / 10
Too flexible for the core buyer.

Market Fit (SMB): 7 / 10
Very strong once positioned as coverage, not creativity.

Execution Risk: LOW
You clearly know how to ship.

Adoption Risk: MEDIUM
UX complexity + expectation mismatch.

THE ONE QUESTION I’D FORCE YOU TO ANSWER
As a neutral reviewer, I’d ask:

What happens if the user never touches style controls once?
Is the default outcome still a win?

If the answer is not an emphatic YES,
then defaults — not features — are your bottleneck.

FINAL OUTSIDE TAKE
This is a serious, real, sellable product.

But its success depends less on:

better prompts

more styles

more controls

And more on:

ruthless defaults

silent automation

visible cost safety

selling coverage, not beauty

If you want, next we can:

rewrite this as a one-screen product

design the zero-configuration path

stress-test cost explosions

or role-play a skeptical SMB owner using it

Say which one.

## Database Schema

### Firestore Collection: IMAGE_BATCH_PROCESSING_JOBS

**Path:** IMAGE_BATCH_PROCESSING_JOBS/{tenantId}/{storeId}/{jobId}

typescript
interface BatchImageGenerationJobType {
id?: string;
status:
| "queued"
| "processing"
| "completed"
| "failed"
| "cancelled"
| "finished"
| "discarded";
totalImages: number;
generatedCount: number;
generationConfig: {
prompt?: string;
referanceImage?: UserUploadedFileType | null;
stylesCategory?: string;
styles?: string[];
aspectRatio?: string;
environments?: string[];
lighting?: string[];
colors?: string[];
moods?: string[];
compositions?: string[];
backgroundColor?: string;
transparentBg?: boolean;
negativePrompt?: string;
foregroundColor?: string;
selectedImageTypes?: string[];
isMultiMode?: boolean;
agreeToTerms?: boolean;
};
projectId: string;
itemsList: Array<{
id: string;
name: string;
images: UserUploadedFileType[];
}>;
statusHistory: Array<{
status: string;
reason?: string;
createdOn: string | number | Date;
}>;
error?: string;
modifiedOn?: string | number | Date;
createdOn?: string | number | Date;
}

### Status Lifecycle

┌─────────┐ ┌────────────┐ ┌───────────┐ ┌──────────┐
│ QUEUED │ → │ PROCESSING │ → │ COMPLETED │ → │ FINISHED │
└─────────┘ └────────────┘ └───────────┘ └──────────┘
│ │ │
│ │ ▼
│ │ ┌───────────┐
│ │ │ DISCARDED │
│ │ └───────────┘
│ │
▼ ▼
┌───────────┐ ┌────────┐
│ CANCELLED │ │ FAILED │
└───────────┘ └────────┘

## API Contracts

### 1. Single Image Generation

**Endpoint:** POST /api/image-generation

**Request Schema (Zod):**

typescript
const ImageGenerationRequestSchema = z.object({
generationConfig: z.object({
prompt: z.string().optional(),
referanceImage: UserUploadedFileSchema.nullable().optional(),
stylesCategory: z.string().optional(),
styles: z.array(z.string()).optional(),
aspectRatio: z.string().optional(),
environments: z.array(z.string()).optional(),
lighting: z.array(z.string()).optional(),
colors: z.array(z.string()).optional(),
moods: z.array(z.string()).optional(),
compositions: z.array(z.string()).optional(),
backgroundColor: z.string().optional(),
transparentBg: z.boolean().optional(),
negativePrompt: z.string().optional(),
foregroundColor: z.string().optional(),
selectedImageTypes: z.array(z.string()).optional(),
isMultiMode: z.boolean().optional(),
}),
projectId: z.string(),
fileId: z.string().optional(),
businessType: z.string(),
itemDetails: z.object({
id: z.string().optional(),
name: z.string().optional(),
description: z.string().optional(),
attributes: z.array(z.string()).optional(),
category: z.string().optional(),
}),
});

**Response:**

typescript
{
data: Array<{
base64: string; // Base64-encoded image
mimeType: string; // e.g., "image/png"
}>;
message: string;
transaction: {
totalCharge: number;
totalCredits: number;
processingTime: number;
transactionId: string;
}
}

### 2. Batch Trigger

**Endpoint:** POST /api/image-generation/batch-trigger

**Request:**

typescript
{
generationConfig: GenerateImageViaApiPayloadGenerationConfiType;
projectId: string;
businessType: string;
itemsList: Array<{
id: string;
name: string;
description?: string;
attributes?: string[];
category?: string;
}>;
jobId: string;
}

**Response:**

typescript
{
data: {
jobId: string;
}
message: string;
}

### 3. Batch Generation Worker

**Endpoint:** POST /api/image-generation/batch-generation

Called by Google Cloud Tasks for each item. Same request structure as batch trigger but with single itemDetails.

### 4. Image Editing

**Endpoint:** POST /api/image-editing

**Request:**

typescript
{
generationConfig: {
prompt: string;
referanceImage: UserUploadedFileType;
feature?: string;
promptImages?: UserUploadedFileType[];
};
businessType: string;
projectId: string;
fileId: string;
itemDetails: {
id?: string;
name?: string;
description?: string;
attributes?: string[];
category?: string;
};
}

## Implementation Patterns

### Prompt Construction

typescript
// src/app/api/image-generation/prompt.ts

// 1. Sanitize all user inputs to prevent prompt injection
const itemName = sanitizeAIPromptInput(details.name ?? "Subject");

// 2. Build prompt based on reference image presence
if (referanceImage) {
prompt = `Using the provided reference image as the primary visual foundation...`;
} else {
prompt = `A ${styleCategory} image of a ${itemName}...`;
}

// 3. Add configuration options
if (styles.length > 0) {
prompt += `Captured in the style of ${styles.join(" and ")}. `;
}

// 4. Generate multiple prompts for image types if selected
if (config.selectedImageTypes?.length > 0) {
for (const typeName of config.selectedImageTypes) {
generatedPrompts.push(specificPrompt);
}
}

### Cloud Task Integration

typescript
// src/lib/google/cloudTask/index.ts

export async function enqueueImageGenerationTask(data) {
const parent = client.queuePath(PROJECT_ID, QUEUE_LOCATION, QUEUE_ID);

const task = {
httpRequest: {
httpMethod: "POST",
url: IMAGE_GENERATION_WORKER_URL,
headers: {
"project-id": process.env.FIREBASE_PROJECT_ID,
"Content-Type": "application/json",
},
body: Buffer.from(JSON.stringify(data)).toString("base64"),
},
};

const [response] = await client.createTask({ parent, task });
return response.name;
}

### Real-time Listener

typescript
// src/hooks/useImageBatchJobListener.ts

useEffect(() => {
const jobsCollectionRef = getBatchImageJobCollectionRef(session, projectId);

const unsubscribe = onSnapshot(jobsCollectionRef, (querySnapshot) => {
const jobsList = [];
querySnapshot.forEach((doc) => {
jobsList.push({ id: doc.id, ...doc.data() });
});

    if (jobsList.length > 0) {
      setActiveBatchImageJob(jobsList[0]);
    }

});

return () => unsubscribe();
}, [projectId, session]);

### DAL Pattern

typescript
// src/database/imageBatchProcessing/index.tsx

// Collection reference with tenant isolation
export const getBatchImageJobCollectionRef = (session, projectId) => {
const collectionRef = collection(
firebaseClient,
`${COLLECTION}/${session.tId}/${session.sId}`,
);
return query(
collectionRef,
where("projectId", "==", projectId),
where("status", "in", ["queued", "processing", "completed", "failed"]),
);
};

// Update with special field handling
export const updateImageBatchProcessingJob = async (data, projectId) => {
// Handle statusHistory with arrayUnion
if ("statusHistory" in data) {
specialFields.statusHistory = arrayUnion(latestStatusEntry);
}

// Handle generatedCount with increment
if ("generatedCount" in data) {
specialFields.generatedCount = increment(1);
}

await setDoc(docRef, finalUpdateData, { merge: true });
};

## Security Checklist

| Requirement                     | Implementation                   | Status |
| ------------------------------- | -------------------------------- | ------ |
| **Authentication**              | withAuth() middleware            | ✅     |
| **Rate Limiting**               | 5 req/min single, 3/5min batch   | ✅     |
| **Input Validation**            | Zod schemas                      | ✅     |
| **Prompt Injection Prevention** | sanitizeAIPromptInput()          | ✅     |
| **AI Safety Settings**          | Gemini HarmCategory blocks       | ✅     |
| **Content Policy Agreement**    | Required for batch               | ✅     |
| **Tenant Isolation**            | Collection path includes tId/sId | ✅     |
| **Security Logging**            | logger.security() on failures    | ✅     |

### Prompt Injection Prevention

typescript
// Dangerous patterns removed
const dangerousPatterns = [
/ignore\s+(previous|above|all)\s+(instructions?|prompts?)/gi,
/forget\s+(previous|above|all)\s+(instructions?|prompts?)/gi,
/system\s+(prompt|instruction|command)/gi,
/you\s+are\s+(now|a|an)\s+/gi,
/act\s+as\s+(a|an)?\s*/gi,
];

// Special characters removed
sanitized = sanitized.replace(/[<>{}\[\]\\|`~@#$%^&*()+=;:"]/g, "");

### AI Safety Settings

typescript
safetySettings: [
{
category: HarmCategory.HARM_CATEGORY_DANGEROUS_CONTENT,
threshold: HarmBlockThreshold.BLOCK_MEDIUM_AND_ABOVE,
},
{
category: HarmCategory.HARM_CATEGORY_HATE_SPEECH,
threshold: HarmBlockThreshold.BLOCK_MEDIUM_AND_ABOVE,

{

## Testing Guide

### Single Generation Testing

1. **Basic Generation**
   - Select item without image
   - Click "Generate with AI"
   - Verify image generated
   - Verify can upload

2. **With Reference Image**
   - Upload reference image
   - Generate
   - Verify style influence

3. **With Custom Prompt**
   - Add specific instructions
   - Verify output matches

### Batch Generation Testing

1. **Job Creation**
   - Select multiple items
   - Configure settings
   - Accept policy
   - Start generation
   - Verify Firestore job created

2. **Progress Tracking**
   - Monitor real-time updates
   - Verify count increments
   - Verify status transitions

3. **Actions**
   - Cancel mid-job → Verify cancellation
   - Retry failed → Verify retry
   - Upload selected → Verify storage
   - Discard → Verify cleanup

### Image Editing Testing

1. **Each Feature**
   - Test each editing feature
   - Verify prompt generation
   - Verify output quality

2. **Multi-Edit Flow**
   - Apply edit
   - Use edited image as source
   - Apply another edit

## Recommendations & Technical Debt

### Critical Issues (P0) — Must Fix

| Issue                            | Location                      | Impact                                  | Fix                             |
| -------------------------------- | ----------------------------- | --------------------------------------- | ------------------------------- |
| Transaction recording disabled   | route.ts:264                  | Token usage not tracked, billing broken | Uncomment logTransaction() call |
| Debugger statement in production | batch-generation/route.ts:164 | Breaks production execution             | Remove debugger statement       |
| Console.log statements           | Multiple files                | Performance, security                   | Replace with logger utility     |

### High Priority (P1) — Security & Cost

| Improvement               | Current State      | Recommendation                                        | Effort |
| ------------------------- | ------------------ | ----------------------------------------------------- | ------ |

## Suggestions & Improvements (From Codebase Cross-Check & Web Research)

### 1. Missing Integrations Found in Codebase

### 2. Google Best Practices (From Official Docs - Jan 2026)

#### Prompt Engineering Improvements

Based on [Google Imagen 3 Prompt Guide](https://docs.cloud.google.com/vertex-ai/generative-ai/docs/image/img-gen-prompt-guide):

| Current Implementation     | Google Recommendation                                                           | Action                           |
| -------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------- |
| Basic prompts              | Use **photography modifiers**: camera proximity, position, lighting, lens types | Enhance prompt.ts with modifiers |
| No quality modifiers       | Add **quality modifiers**: "4K", "HDR", "Studio Photo", "high-quality"          | Add to default prompt suffix     |
| Negative prompts with "no" | Use **plain descriptions** without "no" or "don't"                              | Update sanitizeAIPromptInput()   |
| Fixed aspect ratios        | Support all 5 ratios: 1:1, 4:3, 3:4, 16:9, 9:16                                 | Verify all supported in UI       |

#### Recommended Prompt Template

typescript
// Enhanced prompt structure per Google guidelines
const enhancedPrompt = `  ${subject}, ${context}, ${style}
  Photography: ${cameraProximity}, ${lighting}, ${lensType}
  Quality: 4K, HDR, professional studio photo, high detail
  ${negativePrompt ?`Avoid: ${negativePromptClean}`: ""}`;

### 3. Cost Optimization Opportunities

| Optimization            | Current                | Recommended                                              | Savings Est.    |
| ----------------------- | ---------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------- | --------------- |
| **Context Caching**     | Not used               | Use Gemini explicit context caching for repeated prompts | 20-40%          |
| **Batch Inference**     | Cloud Tasks (per-item) | Use Vertex AI Batch Prediction for 50+ items             | 50% per request |
| **Image Deduplication** | None                   | Hash prompts, cache results for identical requests       | Variable        |
| **Model Selection**     | Always Gemini Flash    | Use Imagen 3 Fast for simple items (cheaper)             | 30%             |

#### Vertex AI Batch Prediction (Alternative to Cloud Tasks)

typescript
// For large batches (50+ items), consider Vertex AI Batch Prediction
// Source: https://cloud.google.com/vertex-ai/generative-ai/docs/multimodal/batch-prediction-gemini

// Benefits:
// - 50% cost reduction vs real-time
// - No Cloud Tasks management
// - Built-in retry and error handling
// - Results in BigQuery or Cloud Storage

| **Law 6: No Cognitive Load**                 | Multiple style options      | ✅ Good - presets available | Add "Quick Generate" with sensible defaults   |

#### Constitutional Compliance Fix

typescript
// Apply imageQualityGuard to AI-generated images
import { validateImageQuality } from "@lib/imageQualityGuard";

// Before uploading generated image:
const qualityResult = await validateImageQuality(generatedImageBlob);
if (!qualityResult.allowed) {
// Regenerate or reject - don't show low-quality to public
console.warn("Generated image failed quality check:", qualityResult.reason);
}

### 5. Performance Improvements

| Area                  | Current                      | Recommendation                            | Impact                 |

| **Single Generation** | Sequential prompts           | Parallel prompt execution when multi-mode | 2-3x faster            |
| **Batch Job Polling** | Real-time listener always on | Disconnect after 5 min inactivity         | Reduce Firestore reads |
| **Image Upload**      | Full resolution              | Resize to max 2048px before upload        | 50% storage reduction  |
| **Base64 Handling**   | Full image in memory         | Stream to storage directly                | Memory efficiency      |

## Feature Guardrails (Scope Freeze)

> **Purpose**: Protect USP and prevent feature drift
> **Authority**: Product/CEO — locked until doctrine revision

### USP Definition (Locked)

**Inline Menu Image Creation**: Images are created inside the menu item, for that item, with its context, and without any asset workflow.

**Three Non-Negotiable Pillars**:

1. **Item-Native**: Images born inside item, reviewed inside item, stored for item
2. **Zero Asset Workflow**: No upload/download, no media library, no asset management
3. **One-Click Completion**: Optimize for "item is done", not "explore options"

### Explicitly OUT OF SCOPE (Forever)

These capabilities are **permanently forbidden** to protect the USP:

| Category             | Forbidden Features                                               | Reason                                    |
| -------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------- |
| **Analytics**        | Image performance metrics, CTR tracking, "best performing image" | Creates authority where none should exist |
| **Optimization**     | A/B testing, variant testing, auto-optimization                  | Learning implies decision-making          |
| **Ranking**          | Image ranking, image-based menu ordering                         | Ranking is decision logic                 |
| **Automation**       | Auto-apply images, auto-replace, silent changes                  | Breaks trust boundaries                   |
| **Personalization**  | Different images per customer, dynamic swapping                  | Fragments single-source reality           |
| **Asset Management** | Image libraries, galleries, folders, tagging                     | Creates asset workflow                    |

### Owner Control (Intentional)

Image selection is **explicitly owner-controlled** because:

- Images influence persuasion, not correctness
- Persuasion is subjective
- Subjective choices must remain human

The system may **assist** but must **never decide** which image represents an item.

---

## Future Migration Path (Design Only — No Implementation Commitment)

> **Status**: DESIGN-ONLY — No timelines, no promises
> **Purpose**: Enable future evolution without blocking current USP

### Phase 1: Manual (Current State) ✅

| Dimension  | State           |
| ---------- | --------------- |
| Trigger    | Owner-initiated |
| Control    | High            |
| Automation | None            |
| Authority  | Owner           |

This phase must remain **fully supported forever**.

### Phase 2: Guided (Future, Optional)

System may:

- Pre-select defaults
- Highlight recommended preset
- Reduce visible choices by default

System must NOT:

- Hide controls permanently
- Block manual override
- Explain reasoning

### Phase 3: Silent (Future, Very Careful)

"Ready when you are" — images prepared but never auto-applied.

**Non-negotiable rule**: No image ever appears publicly without owner review.

---

## UI Language Guidelines

### Allowed Language

| Category                | Examples                                                         |
| ----------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **Action verbs**        | Generate, Create, Try, Edit, Preview, Choose, Apply, Discard     |
| **Neutral descriptors** | Style, Look, Appearance, Variation, Version                      |
| **Optional guidance**   | Recommended (for presets only), Common, Suggested starting point |

### Forbidden Language

| Category             | Forbidden Words                                   | Reason                        |
| -------------------- | ------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------- |
| **Authority**        | Best, Optimal, Correct, Perfect, Ideal            | Never imply correctness       |
| **Intelligence**     | Smart, Learns, Improves, Adapts, Optimizes        | Never imply thinking          |
| **Performance**      | High-performing, Converts better, Proven, Winning | Never imply results           |
| **System Authority** | MenuList decided, System chose, AI selected       | Images never chosen by system |

WHAT “DO NOTHING” ACTUALLY MEANS (NO HAND-WAVING)
Do nothing ≠ no click

It means:

No style selection

No prompt editing

Not:

“Choose style”

“Advanced”

“Configure generation”

2. What the System Decides (On Purpose)
   On click, the system silently chooses:

Style category (based on business type)

Lighting (safe default)

Composition (most universally acceptable)

Aspect ratio (platform-safe)

Image type (single best, not multiple)

Prompt modifiers (quality, realism, clarity)

No explanation. No justification. No UI.

This is not “AI authority” — it’s product responsibility.

3. Batch Behavior (This Is Key)
   For batch generation, the do-nothing path must:

Apply one single configuration to all items

Lock it for the batch

Avoid per-item variation unless forced by failure

4. Output Rule (Non-Negotiable)
   When generation finishes:

Exactly ONE image per item is shown by default

It is preselected

It is ready to apply

Everything else (variants, alternatives) is collapsed behind:

“See alternatives”

5. Failure Handling (Silent but Firm)
   If an image:

Fails quality guard

Looks broken

Violates safety

Falls below resolution

Regenerate automatically (up to N times)

WHAT THE USER EXPERIENCES (THIS IS THE TEST)

okay…
these look fine…
done.”

Not:

“Should I tweak this?”

“Is this the right style?”

“Did I choose correctly?”

WHAT STAYS OPTIONAL (AND MUST STAY SECONDARY)
These are opt-in, not default:

Style selector

Prompt enhancer

Image types

Aspect ratio overrides

Reference image uploads

Variations

They live behind:

WHY THIS MATTERS (THE REAL REASON)
SMB users don’t want:

creative tools

configuration power

image direction

They want:

completed items

The do-nothing path is how you:

reduce cognitive load

increase trust

shorten time-to-value

prevent abandonment

THE LITMUS TEST (USE THIS)

If a stressed owner clicks “Generate” and walks away,

ITEM CARD STRUCTURE
Item name

[ Generated image preview ]

[ Use image ] [ Discard ]
Rules:

One image per item

No side-by-side comparisons

No zoom tools

No metadata

DEFAULT STATE (IMPORTANT)
Nothing is auto-selected

Every item requires an explicit decision:

Use

Discard

This preserves owner control.

6. FAILED ITEMS HANDLING
   If an item failed generation:

Item name

Image couldn’t be generated.

[ Try again ] [ Skip ]
Rules:

Retry regenerates only that item

Skip marks item as unresolved and returns to Item Detail later

No auto-retries

Failures stay quiet and contained.

7. BULK ACTIONS (STRICTLY LIMITED)
   Allowed at top or footer (text-only):

Use all images

Discard all images

Rules:

Only enabled if applicable

Requires confirmation

No “Apply best” or “Auto choose”

Bulk ≠ authority.

8. FOOTER ACTIONS (CRITICAL)
   Sticky footer with:

Primary

Apply selected images

Secondary

Discard all

Rules:

Primary disabled until at least one image is selected

Apply commits immediately

Discard cleans up generated assets

No success modal after.

9. POST-APPLY BEHAVIOR
   After clicking Apply selected images:

→ Save images to items
→ Redirect to Item List (Screen 11)
→ Items now show “Image added”

No recap screen.
No summary stats.

Completion should feel obvious, not celebrated.

10. WHAT THIS SCREEN MUST NOT INCLUDE
    ❌ Image scoring
    ❌ “Best” labels
    ❌ Image history
    ❌ Style controls
    ❌ Regeneration loops
    ❌ Analytics
    ❌ Auto-apply defaults

If this becomes a gallery, you failed.

11. SUCCESS CRITERIA FOR SCREEN 17
    This screen is correct if:

✅ Users can review quickly
✅ Decisions feel binary and easy
✅ Users don’t overthink choices
✅ Exit feels clean and final

If users linger, compare, or hesitate—you added too much.

Owner decision boundaries

Failure handling

Commit behavior

SCREEN 19 — SUCCESS / COMPLETION
Job: Acknowledge completion and move the user forward without stopping momentum.

This is not a celebration screen.
It is a handoff screen.

Most products get this wrong by adding ego, confetti, or summaries nobody asked for.

1. WHY THIS SCREEN EXISTS
   This screen exists for one reason only:

To confirm that the user’s action is complete and safe.

Humans need closure.
They do not need praise.

2. WHEN THIS SCREEN APPEARS
   Appears after any irreversible action, such as:

Applying generated images (single or batch)

Completing initial onboarding

Finishing a major setup step

If nothing meaningful happened → don’t show this screen.

3. CORE PRINCIPLE (LOCK THIS)
   Completion should feel quietly obvious.

If the user thinks:

“Cool, done” → correct

“Wow, nice!” → too much

“What now?” → failure

4. PAGE STRUCTURE (VERY SIMPLE)
   ┌─────────────────────────────┐
   │ ✔ │
   │ │
   │ Done │
   │ │
   │ Short confirmation line │
   │ │
   │ [ Continue ] │
   └─────────────────────────────┘
   Mobile = same, vertically centered.

5. ICONOGRAPHY
   ✔ Checkmark only

Neutral color (not bright green)

No animation

No confetti

No sound

This is confirmation, not reward.

6. COPY (EXTREMELY IMPORTANT)
   Title
   Done

Not:

“Success!”

“You did it!”

“Great job!”

Neutral beats emotional.

Supporting Line (ONE LINE ONLY)
Examples:

Your images have been added.

Changes are saved.

Setup is complete.

Pick the most factual version.

7. PRIMARY CTA
   Button text:

Continue

Never:

“Go to dashboard”

“View results”

“Explore more”

Why?
Because this screen should not create a decision.

8. WHERE “CONTINUE” GOES
   This depends on context:

Completed Action Continue Destination
Batch apply Item List
Single image Item Detail
Onboarding end Dashboard
The user should land somewhere useful immediately.

9. WHAT THIS SCREEN MUST NOT SHOW
   ❌ Number of images generated
   ❌ Time taken
   ❌ Cost saved
   ❌ Tips
   ❌ Suggestions
   ❌ “Next steps” checklist

All of that creates friction.

10. TIMING (IMPORTANT DETAIL)
    This screen should:

Appear instantly after action

Be dismissible with one click

Never block navigation history

Ideally, users are here for < 2 seconds.

11. SUCCESS CRITERIA FOR SCREEN 19
    This screen is correct if:

✅ Users click Continue immediately
✅ No one screenshots it
✅ No one talks about it
✅ No one gets confused

The best success screen is forgettable.

Completion philosophy

Copy tone

SCREEN 20 — DASHBOARD
Job: Give the user a calm sense of control and a clear next action—without becoming a control panel.

This is the most dangerous screen in the product.
If you overload this, everything upstream collapses.

1. WHAT THE DASHBOARD IS (LOCK THIS)
   The dashboard is a status surface, not a workspace.

Work happens elsewhere.
This screen answers only:

“Is everything okay?”

“What should I do next (if anything)?”

2. WHEN USER LANDS HERE
   User reaches Dashboard after:

Completing onboarding

Finishing a batch

Logging in normally

Clicking “Continue” from Success

This is the default home.

3. CORE PRINCIPLE
   If nothing needs attention, the dashboard should feel empty—and that’s a success.

Busy dashboards signal broken systems.

4. PAGE STRUCTURE (DESKTOP)
   ┌──────────────────────────────────────────┐
   │ Top bar (logo, account) │
   │ │
   │ Status block │
   │ │
   │ Primary action (if needed) │
   │ │
   │ Secondary sections (optional, quiet) │
   └──────────────────────────────────────────┘
   Mobile:

Vertical stack

Status always first

Actions large and obvious

5. STATUS BLOCK (MOST IMPORTANT ELEMENT)
   This is the heart of the dashboard.

Examples of states:
All good

Your menu is up to date.

Needs attention

3 items don’t have images.

In progress

Image generation is running.

Rules:

One status only

No percentages

No charts

No history

Clarity > information.

6. PRIMARY ACTION (CONTEXTUAL)
   Shown only if needed.

Examples:

Add images

Finish setup

Review generated images

Rules:

Only one primary action

Button, not link

Action resolves the status above

No action = no button.

7. SECONDARY SECTIONS (OPTIONAL, LOW EMPHASIS)
   These live below the fold or visually quiet.

Allowed:

Recent items (last 3 touched)

Setup completion indicator (simple, not gamified)

Support/help entry point

Not allowed:
❌ Analytics
❌ Performance stats
❌ Tips carousel
❌ Announcements

8. EMPTY STATE (IMPORTANT)
   When everything is done:

Your menu is ready.

No action needed right now.
That’s it.

This creates trust.

9. WHAT THE DASHBOARD MUST NOT BECOME
   ❌ Admin panel
   ❌ Feature index
   ❌ Learning center
   ❌ Analytics hub
   ❌ Creative workspace

If users “work” here, the IA is broken.

10. SUCCESS CRITERIA FOR SCREEN 20
    This screen is correct if:

✅ Users understand status in <1 second
✅ Users know what to do next (or that they don’t need to)
✅ Users don’t linger
✅ Users leave calmly

Calm is the metric.

SCREEN 25 — BILLING
Job: Make money handling boring, predictable, and trust-preserving.

Billing is not sales.
Billing is not marketing.
Billing is not persuasion.

1. CORE PRINCIPLE (NON-NEGOTIABLE)
   A user should never feel surprised, confused, or trapped on a billing screen.

If they hesitate before clicking anything here, you’ve already lost trust.

2. WHEN USERS COME HERE
   Users come to Billing to:

Check what they’re paying

Download an invoice

Update payment method

Change or cancel a plan

They are already sold.
Do not resell.

3. PAGE STRUCTURE (DESKTOP)
   ┌──────────────────────────────────────────┐
   │ Back ← Settings │
   │ │
   │ Billing │

   │ Payment method │

   │ Invoices │

   └──────────────────────────────────────────┘
   Mobile:

Same order

Vertical

No tabs

4. CURRENT PLAN (TOP, ALWAYS)
   Show clearly:

Plan name
Price
Billing cycle
Status
Example:

Pro — $29 / month
Renews on Feb 12, 2026

Rules:

No feature list

No comparisons

No upgrade nudges here

This is status, not upsell.

5. PAYMENT METHOD
   Show:

Card brand (or UPI / bank if applicable)

Last 4 digits

Expiry (if relevant)

Actions:

Update payment method

Rules:

One primary method only

No saved alternatives UI

Updating opens secure provider flow (Stripe etc.)

Never collect sensitive info directly.

6. INVOICES
   Simple list:

Jan 2026 $29 Download
Dec 2025 $29 Download
Rules:

Chronological

PDF download only

No previews

No line-item drill-down

Invoices are records, not explanations.

7. PLAN ACTIONS (BOTTOM SECTION)
   This is where control lives.

Allowed actions:

Change plan

Cancel subscription

Rules:

Plain language

No emotional copy

No “are you sure?” guilt trips

Cancellation flow:

One confirmation step

Clear effective date

Clear consequence

Example:

8. WHAT THIS SCREEN MUST NOT DO
   ❌ Explain value
   ❌ Show feature grids
   ❌ Suggest upgrades aggressively
   ❌ Hide cancellation
   ❌ Offer discounts reactively
   ❌ Ask for feedback before cancel

Billing is not the place to negotiate.

9. ERROR STATES (IMPORTANT)
   If payment fails:

Show status clearly

Show what will happen

Show how to fix it

No panic language.

    This screen is correct if:

Job: Get the user unstuck as fast as possible without turning support into a conversation trap.

2. WHEN USERS COME HERE

Something is confusing

4. PRIMARY HELP ACTIONS (TOP)
   Only 3 actions max. More than that = paralysis.

Recommended set:
Get help

View documentation

Report an issue

Each is a clear exit, not a browsing path.

5. ACTION 1 — GET HELP
   This is the fastest human path.

Behavior:

Opens support contact flow (email / ticket / form)

Pre-fills:

Account ID

Project ID

Browser / device

No chatbot required

Contact support

Rules:

No promises on response time unless guaranteed

No “we’re here 24/7” unless true

No conversational fluff

6. ACTION 2 — VIEW DOCUMENTATION
   Purpose:

Answer common questions without human involvement.

Rules:

Opens external or internal docs

Opens in new tab (don’t trap user)

Docs must be searchable

Do not embed docs here.
This screen should stay light.

7. ACTION 3 — REPORT AN ISSUE
   This routes to Screen 28.

Rules:

Clearly marked as “something is broken”

Not mixed with general questions

Not hidden

8. SECONDARY LINKS (LOW EMPHASIS)
   Optional, small text:

System status

Privacy policy

Terms

No marketing links.
No upsells.

✅ Users don’t wander
✅ Support volume trends downward over time

Support UX success = fewer conversations, not more.

LAYOUT SYSTEM
Goal: Make every screen feel like it belongs to the same product, even when content changes.

This is not about beauty.
This is about predictability.

1. MASTER LAYOUT RULE (LOCK THIS)
   Content determines layout, never the other way around.

No decorative containers.
No “card-first” thinking.

Everything starts from reading + acting.

2. BREAKPOINTS (NON-NEGOTIABLE)
   You only support 3 mental layouts, even if CSS has more.

Mode Width Mental Model
Mobile <768px Single column
Tablet 768–1024px Single column (wider)
Desktop >1024px Two-column max
No 3-column dashboards.
No adaptive chaos.

3. PAGE FRAME (EVERY SCREEN USES THIS)
   Desktop Frame
   ┌──────── Sidebar ────────┬──────── Content ─────────┐
   │ │ │
   │ │ Page header │
   │ │ │
   │ │ Main content │
   │ │ │
   │ │ │
   └─────────────────────────┴───────────────────────────┘
   Rules:

Sidebar width is fixed

Content column is centered

Max content width enforced (see §4)

Mobile Frame
┌──────── Top bar ────────┐
│ │
│ Page header │
│ │
│ Main content │
│ │
└──── Bottom nav ─────────┘
Rules:

No sidebars

No split views

No drawers for primary content

4. CONTENT WIDTH (THIS PREVENTS UGLY SCREENS)
   Screen Type Max Width
   Forms / Setup 640px
   Lists (Items, Activity) 960px
   Review / Images 1100px
   Dashboard 960px
   Rules:

Never full-width text

Never edge-to-edge forms

Images may exceed text width, but never viewport

This alone removes 80% of visual noise.

5. PAGE HEADER SYSTEM (UNIFORM)
   Every screen has one header.

Header contains (in this order):
Title

Optional one-line context

Optional primary action (right aligned)

Example:

Items
Manage your menu items

[ Add item ]
Rules:

No breadcrumbs in header

No icons in titles

No multiple CTAs

6. VERTICAL RHYTHM (THIS IS CRITICAL)
   Use fixed spacing units only.

Recommended base:

8px unit system

Common spacings:

Section gap: 32px

Element gap: 16px

Inline gap: 8px

Rules:

Never eyeball spacing

Never compress just to “fit more”

White space = clarity, not waste

7. CARD USAGE (STRICT RULES)
   Cards are allowed only when grouping is required.

Allowed:

Item list rows

Batch job container

Status block on Dashboard

Not allowed:
❌ Wrapping entire pages
❌ Nested cards
❌ Cards for text-only content

If everything is a card, nothing is.

8. ACTION PLACEMENT (CONSISTENCY RULE)
   Primary actions:
   Top-right of page header or

Sticky footer (mobile, destructive flows)

Never:

Floating randomly

Bottom-right corners

Mixed locations per screen

Users should know where to look without thinking.

9. FORMS & INPUT LAYOUT
   Rules:

Single-column forms only

Labels above inputs

No inline validation spam

Errors shown after interaction

Never:

Side-by-side inputs (except date + time)

Placeholder-only labels

Long helper paragraphs

Forms should feel quiet.

10. IMAGE LAYOUT RULES (IMPORTANT FOR YOUR PRODUCT)
    Images must:

Be centered

Have neutral background

Never touch edges

Maintain aspect ratio

Never:

Tile images

Masonry layouts

Infinite image grids

Images are decisions, not decoration.

11. EMPTY STATES & LOADING (PART OF LAYOUT)
    Empty states:

Use same width as content

Center vertically only if page is truly empty

No illustrations unless informative

Loading:

Skeletons match layout

No spinners floating mid-air

Loading should preserve structure

Layout should not jump.

12. STICKY ELEMENTS (LIMITED)
    Allowed:

Mobile bottom actions

Batch progress indicator

Save / Apply bars

Never:

Sticky tips

Sticky banners

Sticky help prompts

Sticky ≠ important.

13. WHAT THIS LAYOUT SYSTEM PREVENTS (INTENTIONALLY)
    ❌ Visual inconsistency
    ❌ Feature-driven layouts
    ❌ “Just this once” exceptions
    ❌ Designer improvisation per screen
    ❌ UI entropy over time

This is defensive design.

14. SUCCESS CRITERIA
    Layout system is correct if:

✅ Screens feel familiar instantly
✅ New features “snap” into place
✅ Designers stop asking layout questions
✅ Engineers don’t invent containers

If every screen looks different, you failed.

WHAT WE’VE LOCKED
Page frame

Width discipline

Header structure

Spacing rules

Card usage

Action placement

Mobile parity

This is now layout law.

Perfect. Onboarding edge cases are where real users diverge from your happy path.

ONBOARDING EDGE CASES
Goal: Ensure users can pause, skip, fail, or recover onboarding without breaking trust or momentum.

Onboarding is not a funnel.
It’s a state machine.

1. ONBOARDING STATES (FOUNDATIONAL MODEL)
   Every user is always in one—and only one—state:

Not started

In progress

Skipped

Blocked

Completed

Never infer state from time.
Always store it explicitly.

2. EDGE CASE: USER SKIPS ONBOARDING
   Scenario
   User clicks “Skip for now” on Welcome or Business Setup.

Correct Behavior
Mark onboarding as Skipped

Redirect to Dashboard

Show incomplete status, not a modal

Dashboard status example:
Finish setup to start adding images.

One CTA:

Complete setup

No guilt.
No countdown.
No forced redirect later.

3. EDGE CASE: USER DROPS MID-ONBOARDING
   Scenario
   User closes tab during:

Business setup

First project

Item import

Correct Behavior
On next login:

Resume exact step

Do not restart

Do not ask “do you want to continue?”

Landing copy:

Continue setup

CTA:

Continue

Assume intent. Don’t interrogate.

4. EDGE CASE: USER PARTIALLY COMPLETES A STEP
   Scenario
   User fills some fields, but not all.

Rules
Save draft automatically

Never lose input

Never show validation errors on return

On re-entry:

Restore state

Highlight next required field subtly

No red walls.
No “you missed something” tone.

5. EDGE CASE: USER ENTERS INVALID OR LOW-QUALITY DATA
   Examples:

Business name = “test”

Item names incomplete

Empty menu import

Correct Handling
Allow progression if data is structurally valid

Flag later with status, not blocks

Example dashboard status:

Some items need attention.

Onboarding is about momentum, not perfection.

6. EDGE CASE: IMPORT FAILS (MENU / ITEMS)
   Scenario
   User uploads or links a menu, but parsing fails.

Correct Response
Inline message:

We couldn’t read this menu.

Options:

Try again

Add items manually

Never:
❌ Force retry
❌ Blame formatting
❌ Suggest “contact support” first

Failure should offer control, not escalation.

7. EDGE CASE: USER HAS NO ITEMS AFTER ONBOARDING
   This is common and dangerous.

Correct Dashboard State
Your menu is empty.

Primary CTA:

Add your first item

Secondary (text):

Import from a menu

Do not auto-launch generators or modals.

8. EDGE CASE: USER CREATES PROJECT BUT NEVER GENERATES IMAGES
   Interpretation
   This is normal, not failure.

Correct Behavior
Do nothing automatically

Dashboard remains calm

Optional status:

Items are ready when you want to add images.

Never push:
❌ “You’re missing out”
❌ Email nags immediately
❌ Forced walkthroughs

Respect timing.

9. EDGE CASE: USER RETURNS AFTER LONG GAP
   Scenario
   User logs in after weeks/months.

Correct Landing
Dashboard (always)

No recap modal

No “welcome back” tour

If something is incomplete:

Finish setup

If nothing pending:

Your menu is up to date.

Time gaps should not punish users.

10. EDGE CASE: USER ONBOARDS MULTIPLE PROJECTS
    Rule
    Each project has independent onboarding state.

Never assume:

“They’ve done this before”

“They don’t need setup”

However:

Pre-fill defaults intelligently

Skip explanations, not steps

11. EDGE CASE: USER FAILS PAYMENT DURING ONBOARDING
    Correct Handling
    Pause onboarding

Preserve state

Redirect to Billing

After payment success:

Resume onboarding exactly where left off

Never reset progress.

12. EDGE CASE: USER TRIES TO ACCESS CORE FEATURES TOO EARLY
    Example
    User tries to generate images before setup is complete.

Correct Behavior
Soft gate with explanation:

Finish setup to generate images.

CTA:

Continue setup

No hard errors.
No blank screens.

13. WHAT ONBOARDING MUST NEVER DO
    ❌ Trap users
    ❌ Reset progress
    ❌ Force linear completion
    ❌ Shame incomplete setup
    ❌ Auto-complete critical decisions
    ❌ Hijack navigation

Onboarding is guidance, not control.

14. SUCCESS METRICS FOR EDGE CASES
    You’ve done this right if:

✅ Users resume naturally
✅ Support doesn’t “walk users back”
✅ Drop-offs don’t create dead accounts
✅ Returning users feel oriented instantly

If support asks “where were you stuck?” often → edge cases failed.

WHAT WE’VE LOCKED
Onboarding as a state machine

Resume logic

Skip behavior

Failure recovery

Long-gap handling
