ERROR & EMPTY STATES SYSTEM
Goal: When something is missing or broken, the product stays composed and directive.

No drama.
No blame.
No dead ends.

PART A — EMPTY STATES
Core Rule
An empty state is a valid state, not a failure.

If your UI treats emptiness as an error, users feel stupid.

1. TYPES OF EMPTY STATES (ONLY 4 EXIST)
   Do not invent new ones.

1️⃣ First-time Empty
User has never done this before.

Example:

No projects

No items

No images

Tone:

Neutral + inviting

Structure:

What this is

What to do next

One clear action

Example:

No items yet
Add your first item to start generating images
[ Add item ]

2️⃣ Intentional Empty
User deleted or cleared data.

Example:

Cleared items

Discarded batch results

Tone:

Calm + confirming

Example:

No items here
You can add new items anytime

No undo guilt.
No “are you sure?” echo.

3️⃣ Filtered Empty
Data exists, but filters hide it.

Example:

Search with no results

Filtered item list

Tone:

Informational

Example:

No items match your filters
Clear filters to see all items

Never suggest creating new data here.

4️⃣ Transitional Empty
System is working, but not done yet.

Example:

Batch job started, no results yet

Import in progress

Tone:

Reassuring

Example:

Images are being generated
This may take a few minutes

Do not show blank screens while work is happening.

2. EMPTY STATE LAYOUT RULES
   Same width as normal content

Vertically centered only if page is fully empty

No illustrations unless they add meaning

One CTA maximum

Never:
❌ Multiple buttons
❌ Links to docs
❌ Marketing copy

PART B — ERROR STATES
Core Rule
Errors are system failures, not user failures.

Even when users cause them.

3. ERROR SEVERITY LEVELS (STRICT)
   LEVEL 1 — Inline Errors (Recoverable)
   Examples:

Invalid input

Missing required field

Behavior:

Shown near field

Clear correction guidance

No modal

Example:

Item name is required

Never shame:
❌ “You forgot”
❌ “Invalid input detected”

LEVEL 2 — Action Errors (Single Operation Failed)
Examples:

Image generation failed

Import failed

Behavior:

Inline message or toast

Explain what happened

Offer retry or alternative

Example:

Image generation failed
You can try again or edit the prompt

Rules:

Retry must be explicit

Do not auto-retry silently

LEVEL 3 — System Errors (Blocking)
Examples:

API down

Auth expired

Billing blocked

Behavior:

Full-page state

Clear explanation

Single next action

Example:

We couldn’t connect right now
Please try again in a few minutes
[ Retry ]

Never show stack traces or error codes.

LEVEL 4 — Data-Integrity Errors (Rare, Serious)
Examples:

Lost job state

Corrupt batch

Inconsistent data

Behavior:

Acknowledge fault

Preserve user trust

Offer recovery or support

Example:

Something went wrong on our side
Your data is safe
Please contact support with this reference

These should be extremely rare.

4. ERROR COPY RULES (NON-NEGOTIABLE)
   Always:
   Say what happened

Say what user can do next

Use plain language

Never:
❌ Blame the user
❌ Mention “AI failed”
❌ Say “unexpected error”
❌ Expose system internals

Bad:

Something went wrong.

Good:

We couldn’t generate the image.
You can try again or adjust the prompt.

5. ERROR RECOVERY PRINCIPLES
   Errors must:

Preserve user input

Preserve progress

Allow retry without reset

Never:
❌ Clear forms
❌ Reset onboarding
❌ Lose batch selections

Recovery > prevention in user perception.

6. LOADING ≠ ERROR ≠ EMPTY
   Many products confuse these. Don’t.

State User Expectation
Loading “It’s working”
Empty “Nothing here yet”
Error “Something failed”
Each must look and behave differently.

7. GLOBAL ERROR HANDLING (SYSTEM LEVEL)
   Rules:

One global error boundary

Friendly fallback UI

Automatic logging (invisible to user)

Fallback message:

Something didn’t load correctly
Please refresh the page

Do not cascade errors across the app.

8. WHAT THIS SYSTEM PREVENTS
   ❌ Rage clicking
   ❌ Support tickets for “confusion”
   ❌ Users abandoning flows mid-failure
   ❌ Fear of trying again
   ❌ Silent data loss

A calm error state builds more trust than success screens.

SUCCESS CHECKLIST
You’ve done this right if:

✅ Users know what to do next
✅ Errors feel rare, even when frequent
✅ Empty screens don’t feel broken
✅ Support tickets reference clarity, not confusion

If users ask “what should I do now?” → system failed.

WHAT WE’VE LOCKED
Empty state taxonomy

Error severity levels

Copy rules

Recovery principles

Layout discipline

This is now error law.

NEXT
Remaining foundational layer:

Next – Design tokens (colors, typography, components)

This is where visual consistency becomes enforceable.

Say Next when ready.

ChatGPT said:
Good. Design tokens are the enforcement layer.
Without them, everything we defined so far degrades the moment someone ships “just one screen”.

This is where taste becomes law, not opinion.

DESIGN TOKENS SYSTEM
Scope: Colors, Typography, Spacing, Radius, Shadows, Components
Goal: Make inconsistency impossible, not discouraged.

Tokens are inputs, not styles.
No one picks colors. They pick roles.

1. TOKEN PHILOSOPHY (LOCK THIS FIRST)
   Rule 1 — No raw values in UI
   ❌ #000
   ❌ 16px
   ❌ font-weight: 500

✅ text.primary
✅ space.2
✅ font.weight.medium

If a value is used directly, it’s already wrong.

Rule 2 — Tokens represent meaning, not appearance
Bad:

blue500

gray200

Correct:

text.primary

border.subtle

surface.elevated

Meaning survives redesigns. Colors don’t.

2. COLOR TOKENS (MINIMAL, FUNCTIONAL)
   You do not need a palette.
   You need roles.

Core Color Roles
Text
text.primary
text.secondary
text.muted
text.inverse
text.danger
Rules:

No more than 5 text colors

Secondary must pass contrast

Muted never used for actions

Surfaces
surface.page
surface.section
surface.card
surface.overlay
surface.inverse
Rules:

Page ≠ Card ≠ Overlay

Never reuse card color as page background

Overlay is only for modals

Borders
border.default
border.subtle
border.focus
border.danger
Rules:

Borders separate, not decorate

Focus border is the only colored border allowed

Actions
action.primary
action.primary.hover
action.secondary
action.danger
action.disabled
Rules:

Only one primary action per screen

Secondary is visually quieter

Disabled ≠ low opacity text (must still be readable)

Status (limited)
status.success
status.warning
status.error
status.info
Rules:

Used only for system feedback

Never used decoratively

Never combined with primary actions

3. TYPOGRAPHY TOKENS (BORING = GOOD)
   Font Families
   font.family.base
   font.family.mono
   No alternates.
   No expressive fonts.

Font Sizes (MAX 6)
text.xs // helper, meta
text.sm // secondary
text.md // body (default)
text.lg // section headers
text.xl // page titles
text.2xl // rare, marketing only
Rules:

Body is always text.md

No custom sizes

No fluid scaling

Font Weights
font.weight.regular
font.weight.medium
font.weight.semibold
Rules:

No bold everywhere

Semibold only for titles and CTAs

Never mix more than 2 weights on a screen

Line Heights
line.tight // headings
line.normal // body
No per-component tweaking.

4. SPACING TOKENS (THIS ENFORCES RHYTHM)
   Base unit: 8px

space.0 = 0
space.1 = 4
space.2 = 8
space.3 = 16
space.4 = 24
space.5 = 32
space.6 = 48
Rules:

Sections use space.5

Components use space.3

Inline elements use space.2

If spacing looks “off”, it’s because someone broke this.

5. RADIUS TOKENS (NO PERSONALITY HERE)
   radius.none
   radius.sm
   radius.md
   radius.lg
   Usage:

Inputs, buttons → radius.md

Cards → radius.lg

Never mix radii in the same component

No pills.
No circles unless avatar.

6. SHADOW TOKENS (EXTREMELY LIMITED)
   shadow.none
   shadow.sm
   shadow.md
   Rules:

Shadows indicate elevation only

Cards usually do NOT need shadows

Modals use shadow.md

If shadows are visible, you’re probably overusing them.

7. COMPONENT TOKENS (THE REAL POWER)
   Every reusable component has variant tokens, not CSS overrides.

Example: Button
button.primary
button.secondary
button.danger
button.disabled
Each maps internally to:

background

text color

border

hover

focus

disabled

No component accepts raw colors.

Example: Input
input.default
input.focus
input.error
input.disabled
Rules:

Error state overrides everything

Focus is visible but subtle

Disabled never looks broken

8. STATE TOKENS (CROSS-CUTTING)
   Used across all components.

state.hover
state.focus
state.active
state.disabled
state.loading
Rules:

Hover never conveys meaning alone

Focus is keyboard-visible always

Loading disables interaction, not visibility

9. WHAT IS EXPLICITLY FORBIDDEN
   ❌ One-off colors
   ❌ Per-screen typography
   ❌ “Just this once” spacing
   ❌ Inline styles
   ❌ New tokens without system review

If someone needs a new token, the system is missing meaning — not color.

10. GOVERNANCE RULE (IMPORTANT)
    Adding a new token requires:
    What role does this serve?

Is it reusable?

Does it already exist under another name?

If not reusable → do not add.

11. SUCCESS TEST
    Your token system is correct if:

✅ Designers stop asking “what color?”
✅ Engineers stop inventing values
✅ Screens look consistent by default
✅ Redesigns don’t break semantics

If visual QA requires screenshots instead of rules — you failed.

WHAT WE’VE LOCKED
Semantic color roles

Typography discipline

Spacing rhythm

Component variants

Governance model

This is now visual infrastructure, not design taste.

Next – Accessibility rules (contrast, keyboard, ARIA)

3️⃣ MOBILE-FIRST ADAPTATIONS
Goal: Preserve intent, not layout.
Mobile is not “smaller desktop.” It’s lower tolerance.

CORE MOBILE PRINCIPLE (LOCK THIS)
On mobile, the user can only do ONE thing at a time.

If a screen implies multiple simultaneous actions, it’s broken.

1. WHAT COLLAPSES VS WHAT NEVER COLLAPSES
   ❌ MUST COLLAPSE ON MOBILE
   These are secondary by definition:

Sidebar navigation

Filters

Advanced options

Style selectors (long lists)

Batch configuration controls

Secondary metadata

History panels

They become:

Bottom sheets

Progressive disclosure

Inline “Show options” toggles

✅ MUST NEVER COLLAPSE
These are task-critical:

Primary action CTA

Core content (item name, image, status)

Progress indicators (batch, upload)

Error / empty messages

Confirmation actions (Apply, Save, Discard)

If a user has to “open something” to act → bad mobile UX.

2. MOBILE NAVIGATION MODEL (STRICT)
   Use BOTTOM NAV only for:
   Dashboard

Items

Activity / History

Settings

Rules:

Max 4 items

Icon + label

No badges unless critical

Everything else is in-flow navigation, not nav.

Never:
❌ Hamburger for primary navigation
❌ Nested nav levels
❌ Icon-only nav

3. MOBILE HEADER RULES
   Mobile header contains ONLY ONE OF THESE:

Screen title

Back action

Primary action (rare)

Never all three together.

Correct patterns:

Title + Back

Title only

Back + Action (review flows)

If you need more → redesign the screen.

4. MOBILE ACTION PLACEMENT (VERY IMPORTANT)
   Primary actions must be:
   Sticky bottom bar OR

Fixed inline at end of content

Example:

[ Generate image ]
Rules:

Always reachable by thumb

Never hidden behind keyboard

Never scroll-dependent

5. FORMS ON MOBILE
   Rules:

One field per row

No side-by-side inputs

Labels always visible

Keyboard-aware scrolling

Validation:

After blur

Inline

Never modal

If typing feels “claustrophobic,” you’ve overpacked.

6. IMAGE HANDLING ON MOBILE (YOUR CORE RISK AREA)
   Display rules:
   One image at a time

Centered

Neutral background

Aspect ratio preserved

Interaction rules:
Tap to preview fullscreen

Swipe between variations

One confirm action

Never:
❌ Image grids
❌ Side-by-side comparisons
❌ Masonry previews

Mobile is for choosing, not comparing.

7. BATCH FLOWS ON MOBILE (CRITICAL)
   Batch ≠ configuration-heavy on mobile.

Correct mobile batch model:
Select items

Confirm settings (summary only)

Start batch

View progress

Review one by one

Rules:

No long configuration forms

No advanced toggles by default

No inline batch analytics

If batch setup needs scrolling → you failed.

8. PROGRESS & FEEDBACK ON MOBILE
   Always visible:

Loading states

Progress percentage

Current item

Never:
❌ Hidden spinners
❌ Silent background work
❌ Toast-only progress

Mobile users don’t trust invisible work.

9. MODALS & BOTTOM SHEETS (STRICT USAGE)
   Use bottom sheets for:
   Filters

Style selection

Secondary options

Use full-screen modals for:
Critical decisions

Image review

Editing flows

Never stack modals.
Never modal-inside-modal.

10. WHAT MOBILE MUST FEEL LIKE
    Mobile UX should feel:

Calm

Linear

Finishable with one thumb

Impossible to “get lost”

If a user asks “where am I?” → mobile failed.

11. WHAT THIS PREVENTS
    ❌ Desktop-first shrinkage
    ❌ Overloaded screens
    ❌ Hidden primary actions
    ❌ Gesture confusion
    ❌ Rage scrolling

This is defensive mobile design.

MOBILE SUCCESS TEST
You’re done if:

✅ User can complete core task in one hand
✅ Primary action is always visible
✅ No screen needs explanation
✅ No accidental taps matter

What collapses vs stays

Bottom navigation discipline

Action placement rules

Image behavior on mobile

COPY SYSTEM
Scope: Tone, verbs, confirmations, errors, empty states
Goal: Make the product sound calm, competent, and predictable — always.

Copy is behavioral infrastructure.

1. CORE COPY PRINCIPLES (NON-NEGOTIABLE)
   Principle 1 — Copy describes reality, never intention
   Never imply:

Intelligence

Judgment

Optimization

Learning

Authority

The system does, it never decides.

Principle 2 — Verbs > adjectives
Bad:

Smart image generation

Good:

Generate images

Adjectives inflate.
Verbs move.

Principle 3 — One sentence = one job
If a sentence tries to:

Explain

Reassure

Sell
at the same time — it fails.

2. TONE RULES (LOCK THIS)
   Your tone is:

Calm

Neutral

Direct

Unemotional

Adult

You are NOT:
❌ Friendly
❌ Excited
❌ Playful
❌ Impressed with yourself

This is a tool, not a companion.

3. VERB SYSTEM (THIS IS HUGE)
   All actions must come from a finite verb set.

Primary Action Verbs (Allowed)
Action Type Verbs
Create Add, Create, Generate
Modify Edit, Update, Replace
Decide Choose, Select, Apply
Control Start, Stop, Cancel
Cleanup Discard, Remove, Delete
Navigation View, Open, Continue
If a verb is not on this list, question it.

Explicitly Forbidden Verbs
❌ Optimize
❌ Improve
❌ Learn
❌ Analyze
❌ Decide
❌ Recommend
❌ Auto-select
❌ Best

These imply authority or intelligence.

4. BUTTON COPY RULES
   Buttons must:
   Start with a verb

Be ≤ 3 words

Describe the immediate outcome

Good:

Generate image

Add item

Start batch

Apply image

Bad:

Let’s go

Try it out

Continue setup now

Yes, do it

Buttons are contracts, not encouragements.

5. CONFIRMATION COPY (CRITICAL TRUST ZONE)
   Every confirmation must answer exactly 3 questions:

What just happened?

What changed?

What can I do next?

Example — Image applied
Good:

Image applied
This image is now used for this item.

Optional CTA:

Change image

Bad:

Success!
Your image has been successfully processed 🎉

Celebration destroys trust.

6. DESTRUCTIVE ACTION COPY
   Rule
   Destructive actions must be:

Explicit

Calm

Reversible if possible

Example — Discard image
Title:

Discard image?

Body:

This image will be removed from this item.

Actions:

Discard image (primary)

Keep image (secondary)

Never:
❌ “Are you sure?” alone
❌ Red drama language
❌ Capital letters

7. ERROR COPY (STRICT TEMPLATE)
   Every error follows this structure:

What happened.
What you can do next.
Example — Generation failed
Good:

Image generation failed.
You can try again or adjust the prompt.

Bad:

Something went wrong. Please try again later.

Never mention:

AI

System internals

Error codes

Blame

8. EMPTY STATE COPY
   Empty copy must:

Name the state

Offer next action

Avoid implication of failure

Example — No items
Good:

No items yet
Add an item to start generating images.

Bad:

You haven’t added anything 😕

No judgment. Ever.

9. PROGRESS & STATUS COPY
   Rules:
   Present tense

No percentages unless meaningful

No promises

Good:

Generating images
This may take a few minutes.

Bad:

Almost there!
Hang tight 🚀

10. ONBOARDING COPY (SUBTLE BUT FIRM)
    Onboarding copy must:

State what’s needed

Explain why briefly

Offer escape

Example:

Set up your business
This helps organize your items correctly.

CTA:

Continue
Secondary:
Skip for now

Never:
❌ “You must”
❌ “Required to continue”
❌ Fear-based language

11. GLOBAL PHRASES (STANDARDIZE THESE)
    Lock these phrases globally:

Use case Phrase
Resume Continue
Retry Try again
Abort Cancel
Confirm Apply
Remove Discard
Success Applied / Saved / Updated
If different screens say different things — users lose confidence.

12. WHAT THIS COPY SYSTEM PREVENTS
    ❌ Authority leakage
    ❌ Emotional manipulation
    ❌ Confusion after actions
    ❌ Support tickets asking “did it work?”
    ❌ Inconsistent tone across screens

Copy is how the system keeps its promises.

SUCCESS TEST
You’ve done this right if:

✅ Screens can be read without UI
✅ No copy feels surprising
✅ Actions feel predictable
✅ Users trust outcomes without explanation

If users reread copy to be sure — you failed.

Tone discipline

Verb whitelist

Button contracts

Confirmation structure

Error templates

Destructive language rules

This completes the foundational UX system.

If you want to go further:

Accessibility enforcement (not guidelines, rules)

QA checklist for new screens

“Reject checklist” for future features

Good. Now we lock interaction rules — the invisible layer that decides whether the product feels solid or sloppy.

INTERACTION MICRO-RULES
Scope: Timing, transitions, feedback, focus, interrupts
Goal: Make every interaction feel deliberate and predictable.

This is behavioral physics. Break it and users lose trust fast.

1. RESPONSE TIME RULES (HARD BOUNDS)
   Rule 1 — Instant feedback, delayed result
   Every action must acknowledge within 100–300ms, even if work continues.

Bad:

Click → nothing → spinner appears late

Good:

Click → state change immediately → background work continues

Allowed immediate feedback:
Button state change

Inline status text

Spinner placeholder

Disabled button

Rule 2 — Never fake speed
If something takes time, say so calmly.

Allowed:

This may take a few minutes.

Forbidden:
❌ “Almost done”
❌ Animated optimism
❌ Progress bars without real meaning

2. LOADING & PROGRESS STATES
   Three loading tiers (do not mix)
   Duration Treatment
   < 300ms No loader (instant UI response only)
   300ms–3s Inline spinner + text
   > 3s Dedicated progress state
   > Never show spinners for fast actions — it signals instability.

Batch / long tasks
Rules:

Show what is happening, not percentage

Always show escape

Example:

Generating images
You can continue working while this runs.

CTA:

View progress
Cancel

3. DISABLED STATES (VERY IMPORTANT)
   Disabled does not mean hidden.

When to disable:
Missing required input

Action already in progress

Permission denied

Disabled button rules:
Keep visible

Show tooltip explaining why

Never animate disabled elements

Bad:
❌ Button disappears
❌ Button shakes
❌ Button turns into spinner without explanation

4. FOCUS & ATTENTION CONTROL
   Rule: The system never steals focus
   Focus only moves when:

User triggers it

A modal opens

A critical error occurs

Never:
❌ Auto-scroll
❌ Jump content
❌ Reorder lists live

If content updates in background → badge it, don’t move it.

5. MODALS & OVERLAYS (STRICT)
   Modal rules:
   One purpose only

One primary action

One safe exit

Modal must answer:

What is this?

What can I do?

How do I leave?

Never nest modals. Ever.

If you need nesting → your screen design is wrong.

6. CONFIRMATION TIMING
   Confirm after state change, not before — except destructive actions.

Correct pattern:
Action → Apply → Confirmation appears

Wrong pattern:
Click → Are you sure? → Click again → Nothing obvious happens

Confirmations should feel like receipts, not checkpoints.

7. INTERRUPTS & ERRORS
   Rule: Errors must not reset progress
   If an error occurs:

Preserve user input

Preserve scroll position

Preserve selections

Resetting state = punishment.

Error visibility
Inline for local issues

Banner for page-level issues

Modal only for blocking failures

Never stack errors.

8. TRANSITIONS & ANIMATION RULES
   Animation exists only to:

Explain change

Preserve context

Allowed animations:
Fade in/out (100–150ms)

Height expand/collapse

Subtle slide (≤ 8px)

Forbidden:
❌ Bounce
❌ Elastic easing
❌ Celebration effects
❌ Infinite animations

If animation draws attention to itself → remove it.

9. EMPTY → FILLED TRANSITION
   When empty state becomes filled:

Replace content in-place

No full page reload

No congratulatory UI

Example:
Empty list → item appears → subtle highlight (1s max)

This teaches cause → effect.

10. ESCAPE HATCH RULE (CRITICAL)
    Every long-running or risky action must have an escape.

Action Type Escape
Batch job Cancel
Modal Close
Edit Discard changes
Onboarding Skip for now
No dead ends. Ever.

11. GLOBAL CONSISTENCY RULES
    These must behave identically everywhere:

Save

Cancel

Back

Apply

Discard

If one screen violates this, users hesitate everywhere.

12. INTERACTION QA CHECKLIST (USE THIS)
    Before shipping any screen, ask:

Did the UI respond immediately?

Can the user escape every flow?

Did focus stay where expected?

Were errors recoverable?

Did anything move without consent?

Could this work without animations?

If any answer is “no” → fix before shipping.

WHAT WE’VE NOW COMPLETED
✅ Screen inventory
✅ Screen wireframes
✅ Navigation & layout system
✅ Mobile-first rules
✅ Copy system
✅ Interaction micro-rules

You now have a full UX operating system, not screens.

Define accessibility as rules (not guidelines)

Create a UX rejection checklist for future features

1️⃣ COST AWARENESS UX
Scope: Single image + Batch

CORE PRINCIPLES (LOCK THESE FIRST)
Principle 1 — Inform, don’t ask permission
Never:

Gate actions behind cost confirmation

Force “Are you sure?” for normal usage

Cost ≠ danger.

Principle 2 — Show cost before commitment, not before intent
Meaning:

Don’t show cost on idle screens

Show cost after configuration, before execution

Principle 3 — Precision scales with magnitude
Small actions → soft estimate

Large actions → clearer estimate

Never show false precision

A. SINGLE IMAGE GENERATION — COST UX
Where cost appears
Inside the generation modal, below the primary button.

Not global.
Not hidden.
Not upfront.

Default state (before any input)
Nothing shown.

No cost text at all.

Why:

Avoid cognitive noise

Avoid anchoring fear

When cost becomes visible
Triggered when Generate image becomes enabled.

Cost copy (single image)

Optional helper (tooltip on hover ⓘ):

Based on one image generation.

That’s it.

No currency.
No credits.
No decimals.

Why?

Single image cost is not decision-worthy

Precision here adds fear, not clarity

During generation
No cost updates.
No running tally.

Cost is not a progress metric.

• ~50 image generations
• Moderate usage

❌ Credits remaining

❌ API jargon

Those belong in Billing, not flow execution.

3. ESCALATION THRESHOLD (IMPORTANT)
   When batch size crosses a defined threshold (example: >30 items):

Add a passive emphasis, not a blocker.

• Higher usage

Neutral warning icon (not red)

No modal

No confirmation step

Not:

❌ “Proceed with cost”

D. IN-PROGRESS COST UX (INTENTIONALLY SILENT)
During batch execution:

Show running cost

Show credits draining

Show warnings mid-run

Batch = fire-and-forget.

2️⃣ BATCH RETRY UX
Scope: Partial retry, full retry
Goal: Let users recover failures without confusion, repetition, or fear.

CORE PRINCIPLES (NON-NEGOTIABLE)
Principle 1 — Retry is explicit, never automatic
If the system retries on its own:

Users lose cost predictability

Users lose control

All retries are user-initiated.

Principle 2 — Retry scope must be visible
Users must always know:

What will retry

What will not

What stays untouched

No ambiguity.

Principle 3 — Retry never resets success
Successful items are locked unless user explicitly changes them.

A. WHERE RETRY LIVES (IMPORTANT)
Retry is only available in:

Batch Review Screen

Not:
❌ Batch progress
❌ Dashboard
❌ History (history is read-only)

This keeps retry close to context.

B. BATCH REVIEW — STATUS SEGMENTATION
At the top of Batch Review:

Batch completed
• 42 completed
• 6 failed
Each item row shows one of three states:

State Label
Success Generated
Failed Failed
Pending (if any) Not generated
No icons with meaning. Text only.

C. PRIMARY RETRY ENTRY POINT
If any failures exist, show a retry block:

Some items were not generated.
Primary action:

Retry failed items

Secondary:

Retry all items

Why both?

Most users want partial

Full retry must still be possible

D. PARTIAL RETRY (DEFAULT PATH)
On click: “Retry failed items”
Open a light confirmation panel (not modal):

Title:

Retry failed items

Body:

6 items will be retried.
Generated images will remain unchanged.

Actions:

Retry failed items (primary)

Cancel

No cost shown here again
(cost was already surfaced earlier).

What happens next
New batch job is created

Scope = failed item IDs only

Status returns to Batch Progress

Status text:

Retrying 6 items

E. FULL RETRY (DANGEROUS — HANDLE CAREFULLY)
On click: “Retry all items”
This does require a modal, because it can overwrite intent.

Full Retry Confirmation Modal
Title:

Retry all items?

Body:

This will generate images again for all 48 items.
Existing generated images will not be removed.

Actions:

Retry all items (primary)

Cancel

Why this wording:

No threat language

No cost panic

Clear scope

F. RETRY FAILURE HANDLING
If retry also fails:

Inline message:

Some items could not be generated again.

Actions:

Retry failed items

Adjust options

No infinite loop prompts.

After 2 consecutive failures, suggest adjustment:

You may want to adjust the prompt or options.

That’s it.

G. WHAT RETRY DOES NOT DO
Retry must NOT:

❌ Reset successful items
❌ Delete previews
❌ Auto-apply images
❌ Change generation settings silently
❌ Merge history entries

Each retry = new batch job.

H. HISTORY IMPLICATION (IMPORTANT)
History records:

Original batch

Each retry as a separate entry

Linked via parentBatchId

UI implication:

“Retry of Batch #123”

But:
❌ No retry actions in History screen

History is evidence, not control.

I. EDGE CASES (COVER THESE)
Case 1 — User leaves mid-retry
On return:

Retry in progress
3 of 6 completed

Actions:

View progress

Cancel retry

Case 2 — User edits items before retry
Block retry with inline notice:

Some items were changed.
Update selection to retry.

CTA:

Update selection

No auto-sync.

Case 3 — Zero failures
Retry UI must not appear.

Silence is correctness.

J. QA CHECKLIST — BATCH RETRY
This passes if:

✅ User can retry only failed items
✅ Successful items are never touched
✅ Full retry is clearly scoped
✅ Retry doesn’t feel like a penalty
✅ History remains truthful
✅ No surprise cost escalation

If users ask support

“Did it redo everything?”
you failed.

WHAT WE JUST LOCKED
Retry location

Partial vs full retry distinction

Confirmation language

Failure loop limits

History semantics

This makes batch recoverable without drama.

3️⃣ DRAFT & RESUME STATES
Scope: Single image, Batch generation
Goal: Users can leave, return, refresh, or crash — without losing work or trust.

Principle 2 — Resume must be obvious, not clever
Users should recognize what’s happening instantly.

No timelines.
No mystery states.

Principle 3 — Never auto-resume without consent
System may detect drafts, but user chooses whether to continue.

A. SINGLE IMAGE — DRAFT STATES

A draft exists if:

Image was generated

Image was NOT applied

That’s it.

Item Detail → Image slot

[ Image preview ]
Draft
Not a badge.
Not colored.
Just text under the image.

User actions on draft
Actions visible:

Generate another

Discard

No automatic prompts.

Leaving the page

No modal.

Draft remains.

We do NOT nag.

B. RESUMING SINGLE IMAGE DRAFT
On return to Item Detail:

User sees:

The draft image

Same actions as before

No explanation text.

Recognition > explanation.

C. BATCH — DRAFT & IN-PROGRESS STATES

1. Batch Created, Not Started
   Status:

Batch Setup screen

Start batch

Edit options

Discard batch

Cancel

3. Batch Completed, Not Reviewed

Review images before applying.

Primary:

Secondary:

Later

No auto-open.
No forced review.

D. RESUME ENTRY POINTS (WHERE USERS SEE THIS)
Resume signals appear in only three places:

Dashboard (top)

Relevant item list row

Batch setup / review screens

Never everywhere.

Too much resume UI = anxiety.

E. DISCARDING DRAFTS (INTENTIONAL)
Single image discard
Already covered — user-triggered only.

Batch discard
Only allowed when:

Keep batch

F. TIMEOUT & STALE DRAFT RULES

Archive silently

Nothing else.

❌ Force decisions on load

Where drafts live

When user decides

How cleanup behaves

4️⃣ IMAGE STATUS INDICATOR UX
Scope: Item-level images (single + batch)
Goal: Make image state obvious without creating an asset system.

This is state clarity, not management.

CORE PRINCIPLES (LOCK THESE)
Principle 1 — Status is descriptive, not evaluative
Status answers “what is this?”, not “is it good?”

Never imply:
❌ Quality
❌ Performance
❌ Recommendation

Principle 2 — Status is local to the item
No global gallery.
No image library.
No reuse UI.

Image truth lives with the item.

Principle 3 — One image = one state
No dual states.
No overlaps.
No ambiguity.

A. CANONICAL IMAGE STATES (FINAL LIST)
There are exactly 4 allowed states.

State Meaning
Live This image is currently used for the item
Draft Generated but not applied
Generating In progress
Failed Last generation attempt failed
Nothing else.
No “pending”, no “reviewing”, no “approved”.

B. WHERE STATUS IS SHOWN
Status appears in two places only:

Item Detail (primary)

Item List (secondary, compact)

Never in:
❌ Dashboard summaries
❌ History tables
❌ Batch progress rows

C. ITEM DETAIL — STATUS DISPLAY
Layout (below image)
[ Image preview ]
Live
Text-only.
No badge.
No color.
No icon.

Why:

Badges invite interpretation

Text is unambiguous

State-specific actions

1. Live
   Actions:

Change image

Edit image

No regenerate button here — regenerate = change.

2. Draft
   Draft
   Actions:

Apply image (primary)

Generate another

Discard

3. Generating
   Generating
   Actions:

Cancel

Nothing else.

4. Failed
   Failed
   Actions:

Try again

Edit options

No auto retry.

D. ITEM LIST — STATUS (COMPACT)
In Item List, show status only if not Live.

Example row:

Pasta Alfredo Draft
If Live → show nothing.

Why:

Live is default expectation

Only exceptions need visibility

E. BATCH CONTEXT — STATUS BEHAVIOR
Batch operations update item statuses, not create new concepts.

Examples:

Batch generates image → item becomes Draft

Batch applies images → items become Live

Batch fails → affected items show Failed

Batch never introduces its own image state.

F. STATUS TRANSITION RULES (IMPORTANT)
Only these transitions are allowed:

Generating → Draft
Generating → Failed
Draft → Live
Draft → Discarded (removed)
Live → Draft (when replaced)
Forbidden:
❌ Failed → Live
❌ Generating → Live
❌ Live → Failed

State transitions must match reality.

G. STATUS COPY (LOCKED)
Exact words only:

State Copy
Live Live
Draft Draft
Generating Generating
Failed Failed
No synonyms.
No emojis.
No variation.

H. EDGE CASES
Multiple images generated
Only the currently visible image has a status.

No stack.
No carousel state.

User applies image, then batch retries same item
Item returns to Draft (new image exists).

Never overwrite Live silently.

I. QA CHECKLIST — IMAGE STATUS
This passes if:

✅ User can answer “is this image live?” instantly
✅ Drafts never appear as live
✅ Failures are visible but calm
✅ No new concepts introduced
✅ No asset workflow emerges

If someone asks:

“Which image is being used?”
you failed.

WHAT WE LOCKED
Exact image states

Where status appears

What actions map to each state

Allowed transitions

Copy vocabulary

This prevents silent confusion — the most expensive UX bug.

5️⃣ HISTORY GRANULARITY UX
Scope: Image generation, batch operations, applies, discards
Goal: Users can understand what happened without being invited to optimize, compare, or interfere.

CORE PRINCIPLES (NON-NEGOTIABLE)
Principle 1 — History is read-only
History never:

Triggers actions

Allows retries

Allows edits

Reopens flows

If users can do things from history, you built the wrong screen.

Principle 2 — Log events, not opinions
History records facts, never:
❌ Quality
❌ Success vs failure framing
❌ Performance
❌ Recommendations

Principle 3 — Granularity stops at human decision points
If the system did something internally, it does not belong here.

History only shows things the user caused or confirmed.

A. WHAT HISTORY LOGS (FINAL, LOCKED)
Exactly 6 event types.

# Event When it logs

1 Image generated After generation completes
2 Image applied User applies image
3 Image discarded User discards draft
4 Batch started User starts batch
5 Batch completed Batch finishes (any result)
6 Batch cancelled User cancels batch
Nothing else.
No retries logged as special events (they are just new batches).

B. WHAT HISTORY DOES NOT LOG
Explicitly forbidden:

❌ Prompt text
❌ Style choices
❌ Cost numbers
❌ Retry attempts
❌ Internal failures
❌ Partial progress

Why:

Prompts leak creativity

Costs create anxiety

Failures belong to the moment, not the record

C. HISTORY SCREEN — STRUCTURE
Layout
History
────────────────────────
[ Event list ]
No filters by default.
No charts.
No summaries.

Row format (strict)
Each row shows four fields only:

Image generated
Item: Pasta Alfredo
Today, 2:41 PM
Or for batch:

Batch completed
48 items
Yesterday, 11:12 AM
That’s it.

D. GROUPING RULES
Group by time, not by item
Sections:

Today

Yesterday

Earlier

Never group by:
❌ Item
❌ Batch
❌ Outcome

Time grouping preserves chronology and causality.

E. LINKING BEHAVIOR
Each row may have one optional link:

“View item” (for item events)

“View batch” (for batch events)

Clicking:

Navigates to context

Does NOT replay anything

Does NOT open modals automatically

History points forward, not backward.

F. HISTORY DETAIL VIEW (LIMITED)
If user clicks “View batch”:

They see:

Batch summary

Final counts

Item list with statuses

They do NOT see:
❌ Retry buttons
❌ Cost breakdown
❌ Prompt settings

Retry always happens from Batch Review, not History.

G. RETENTION & CLEANUP (INVISIBLE)
System may:

Auto-trim history after X days

Archive silently

User-facing behavior:

Older activity is no longer shown.

No settings.
No warnings.

H. COPY RULES (LOCKED)
Allowed verbs:

Generated

Applied

Discarded

Started

Completed

Cancelled

Forbidden:
❌ Failed
❌ Optimized
❌ Improved
❌ Selected

Failure is contextual, not historical.

I. QA CHECKLIST — HISTORY
This passes if:

✅ History explains “what happened”
✅ History does not invite optimization
✅ No actions leak into history
✅ Timeline feels calm
✅ No anxiety-inducing data appears

If users ask:

“Can I redo this from history?”
you failed.

WHAT WE LOCKED
Exact event types

Granularity ceiling

Read-only enforcement

Copy vocabulary

Navigation behavior

This keeps authority where it belongs: the present, not the past.

6️⃣ RESUME AFTER REFRESH / RE-ENTRY UX
Scope: Page refresh, tab close, browser crash, login re-entry
Goal: User never wonders “did I break it?”

This is about continuity of reality.

CORE PRINCIPLES (LOCK THESE)
Principle 1 — Refresh must be harmless
A refresh should feel like:

“I’m back where I left off.”

Not:
❌ “Did it restart?”
❌ “Did I lose something?”

Principle 2 — Resume is surfaced, not forced
The system can detect state, but never assume intent.

No auto-opening modals.
No auto-resuming actions.

Principle 3 — Re-entry cues are contextual
Resume signals appear only where they make sense.

No global panic banners.

A. WHAT COUNTS AS “INTERRUPTION”
An interruption is any of these:

Page refresh

Tab close

Browser crash

Logout / login

Network drop

All are treated identically.

Cancel

No popup.
No alert.

Status: Draft

Same actions as before

No “Resume” CTA needed — visibility is resume.

Item

What problem this solves

Behavior rules (edge cases)
Rule A — Regeneration overrides origin
If user regenerates manually after a batch:

Generated manually
Latest action always wins.

Rule B — Batch retry updates origin
If an item failed in batch and is retried via batch:

Generated via batch
Retry does not change attribution type.

Rule C — Edit does NOT change origin
Editing an image does not change origin.

Origin tracks generation, not modification.

Rule D — Draft images still show origin
Draft state example:

Draft — Generated via batch
Two facts. One line.

❌ “Optimized”
❌ “Suggested”

No intelligence language. No authority language.

Visual priority
Smaller than item title

Same weight as “Draft” label

Never competes with image itself

If a user doesn’t care → it disappears into the background
If a user does care → it’s right there

That’s correct UX.

2️⃣ Concurrent Batch Conflict UX
(Overlapping batches, same items)

The problem (real, not theoretical)
User actions that WILL happen:

Starts Batch A (50 items)

Before it finishes, selects overlapping items

Starts Batch B

If you allow this silently →
race conditions, overwritten drafts, cost disputes, blame.

If you hard-block everything →
UX feels brittle and restrictive.

We need controlled permissiveness.

Core rule (lock this)
An item may belong to only one active batch at a time.

This is item-level, not batch-level.

What counts as “active batch”
An item is locked if it is in a batch with status:

queued

processing

NOT locked if:

completed

failed

cancelled

discarded

UX BEHAVIOR — STEP BY STEP
A. Batch Setup Screen (Primary Control Point)

Visual treatment (inline, no modal)

Checkbox disabled

Item row muted

Small inline note:

In another batch
No explanation text.
No tooltip by default.

Summary line (top of list)
If ≥1 conflicted items detected:

Some items are currently being generated.
That’s it.

No batch IDs.
No timestamps.
No panic language.

B. CTA BEHAVIOR
“Start Batch” button
Enabled only if ≥1 selectable item

Conflicted items are automatically excluded

Button copy does NOT change

We do not say:
❌ “Start partial batch”
❌ “Exclude locked items”

System handles it quietly.

C. What we NEVER do
❌ No confirmation modal
❌ No forced cancel of other batch
❌ No “Replace existing batch?”
❌ No auto-merge

Those are power-user traps.

D. Visibility of the Other Batch
We do not cross-link batches.

User learns about the running batch through:

Dashboard banner

Batch progress screen

Not here.

Batch setup remains focused.

E. Edge Cases (LOCKED)
Rule 1 — Same user, same session
Conflict rules still apply.
No “you started it, so it’s fine”.

Consistency > convenience.

Rule 2 — Different users
Same behavior.
No identity-based exceptions.

Rule 3 — Batch completes while setup is open
On next list refresh:

Disabled items become selectable

No toast

No message

Reality updates quietly.

F. Copy rules (locked)
Allowed:

In another batch

Forbidden:
❌ Locked
❌ Busy
❌ System is using
❌ Please wait
❌ Resolve conflict

No blame. No instructions.

3️⃣ Item Locking During Active Batch
(Editing while generation is in progress)

The risk
If users can freely edit an item while it’s being generated:

Prompts may mismatch item state

Generated images no longer represent the item

Users blame “AI being wrong” when the system was

So we need soft locking, not hard lockdown.

Core rule (lock this)
While an item is in an active batch, its content is editable, but its image actions are frozen.

This preserves momentum without breaking correctness.

WHAT IS LOCKED vs ALLOWED
🔒 Locked during active batch
These actions are disabled:

Generate image (single)

Regenerate image

Edit image

Discard image

Upload image

Basically: anything that touches the image slot.

✅ Still allowed
These remain editable:

Item name

Description

Attributes

Category

Pricing

Textual metadata

Users can keep working.

UX IMPLEMENTATION
A. Item Detail Screen
Image section state
Image slot visible

Buttons disabled

Hover tooltip (ONLY on hover):

Image generation in progress
No permanent text.

Disabled button style
Same buttons

Muted

Non-clickable

No lock icons

No spinners

We don’t dramatize.

B. Inline Status Indicator
Under image slot (same area as origin label):

Generating via batch
This replaces:

Generated via batch
Once completed, it swaps automatically.

C. What happens if user tries anyway
If user clicks a disabled control:

Nothing.

No toast.
No warning.
No modal.

The disabled state is the message.

D. Batch Completion Transition
When batch finishes:

Controls re-enable automatically

Status label updates

No notification needed (banner already exists elsewhere)

E. Edge Cases
Rule 1 — Batch fails
If item fails generation:

Image actions re-enable

Status becomes:

Generation failed
Primary action becomes:

Generate image

No retry here — retry lives in batch review only.

Rule 2 — User refreshes mid-batch
On re-entry:

Same disabled state

Same status label

No restoration message.

Copy rules (locked)
Allowed:

Generating via batch

Image generation in progress

Generation failed

Forbidden:
❌ Locked
❌ Please wait
❌ System busy
❌ We are generating

Language must describe state, not intent.

QA checklist
This passes if:

✅ Users can’t break image correctness
✅ Users aren’t blocked from editing text
✅ No modals interrupt flow
✅ State survives refresh
✅ Image actions re-enable correctly

If a user says:

“Why can’t I edit the image?”

The UI already told them.

If they say:

“I lost my changes”

You failed.

4️⃣ Draft Precedence Rules
(Single vs Batch image drafts)

The real problem
An item can accumulate multiple candidate images:

Single generation → Draft A

Batch generation → Draft B

Retry → Draft C

If precedence isn’t explicit, users will:

Think images “changed by themselves”

Apply the wrong image

Lose confidence

So we define one visible draft, always.

Core rule (lock this)
Only one draft image may be visible per item at any time.

No stacks.
No galleries.
No confusion.

PRECEDENCE ORDER (HARD LOCK)
From highest → lowest priority:

1️⃣ Manual (Single) Draft
2️⃣ Batch Draft (latest)
3️⃣ Existing Applied Image

Manual always wins over batch.

HOW THIS PLAYS OUT
A. Batch generates image for item with no manual draft
Result:

Draft image appears

Label:

Draft — Generated via batch
User can:

Apply

Discard

Edit (once batch finishes)

B. User manually generates image while batch exists
Allowed only if:

Item is NOT in active batch (covered in #3)

Result:

Manual draft replaces batch draft visually

Batch draft is retained in background, but hidden

Label:

Draft — Generated manually
C. Batch finishes AFTER manual draft exists
Behavior:

Batch image is generated

But not surfaced

Manual draft remains visible

No banner.
No toast.
No message.

Batch result is stored but dormant.

D. User discards manual draft
If batch draft exists in background:

Batch draft becomes visible automatically

Label updates accordingly

If not:

Revert to applied image or empty state

This feels natural.

E. Batch retry creates new image
New batch draft replaces older batch draft

Still lower priority than manual

Always latest batch only.

F. What we NEVER expose
❌ Draft gallery
❌ “Other generated images”
❌ Compare view
❌ Image history carousel

Those violate focus and create asset workflows.

STATUS TRANSITION TABLE (REFERENCE)
Situation Visible Image
Only applied image Applied
Batch draft only Batch draft
Manual draft only Manual draft
Manual + batch draft Manual draft
Manual discarded, batch exists Batch draft
All drafts discarded Applied / empty
COPY RULES (LOCKED)
Allowed:

Draft — Generated manually

Draft — Generated via batch

Forbidden:
❌ “Alternative image”
❌ “Option 1 / Option 2”
❌ “System suggestion”
❌ “Best version”

No ranking. No judgment.

QA CHECKLIST
This passes if:

✅ Only one draft visible at a time
✅ Manual actions always feel respected
✅ Batch never silently overrides user intent
✅ Discard behavior feels predictable
✅ No asset-management creep

If a user says:

“Where did my image go?”

You failed.

If they say:

“Oh, that makes sense.”

You won.

Status
✔ Feature #4 LOCKED
✔ No new screens
✔ No comparison UI
✔ Clear backend rule (priority enum)

We design this so that:

v2 (teams) doesn’t require redesign

No authority confusion is introduced

Core rule (lock this)
Permissions are enforced through availability, not explanation.

If someone can’t do something:

The UI shows that clearly

The UI does not justify itself

PERMISSION ROLES (MINIMAL, FUTURE-SAFE)
We assume two roles only for UX purposes:

1️⃣ Owner
2️⃣ Editor / Staff

No admins, no hierarchies, no policy UI.

A. IMAGE ACTION PERMISSIONS (CRITICAL)
Owner can:
Generate images

Batch generate

Apply images

Discard images

Retry batch items

Editor / Staff can:
View images

See drafts

See batch progress

Edit item text

❌ Cannot apply / discard / generate

B. HOW DENIAL IS SHOWN (THIS MATTERS)
Rule: Never hide the capability entirely
Hidden actions create:

Confusion

“Why is this missing?” questions

Instead → visible but disabled.

Example: Item Detail — Image Slot (Editor)
Buttons shown but disabled:

Generate

Edit

Discard

Apply

Hover tooltip (only on hover):

Only the owner can change images
That’s the only explanation.

No modals.
No docs link.
No role names exposed.

C. BATCH SCREENS (Editor VIEW)
Batch Setup
Screen visible

Item list visible

Checkboxes disabled

CTA disabled

Inline text at bottom:

Only the owner can start a batch
No red warnings.

Batch Progress / Review
Fully visible

Read-only

Apply / Retry buttons disabled

Tooltip:

Only the owner can apply changes
D. BILLING & SETTINGS
Billing screen:
Visible

Read-only summary

All CTAs disabled

Tooltip:

Only the owner can manage billing
Settings:
Tabs visible

Editable fields disabled

Same pattern.

E. WHAT WE NEVER DO
❌ Role selector UI
❌ “Request access” buttons
❌ Permission error modals
❌ Login redirects
❌ Silent failures

Those all imply hierarchy management — out of scope.

F. COPY RULES (LOCKED)
Allowed:

Only the owner can …

View only

Read-only

Forbidden:
❌ You don’t have permission
❌ Access denied
❌ Admin required
❌ Contact your administrator

Tone stays calm, factual, non-judgmental.

G. EDGE CASES
Owner logs in as Editor later
UI reflects role immediately

No “role changed” toast

Reality simply updates.

Mixed sessions (two users)
Permissions evaluated per session

No optimistic UI

This passes if:

✅ Editors always understand what they can’t do
✅ Owners never see clutter
✅ No hidden capabilities
✅ No security-through-obscurity
✅ No future UX rework needed for teams

If an editor asks:

The tooltip already answered.

If they ask:

“How do I get access?”

That’s a business process — not a UX failure.

EMPTY STATES — GLOBAL UX SYSTEM (LOCKED)
Why this matters (short, sharp)
Empty states are not “nothing happened yet” screens.
They are instructional surfaces that define:

What the product is

What action is expected next

What the product will not do for you

If you get this wrong, users demand automation, previews, analytics, and hand-holding later.

EMPTY STATE RULES (GLOBAL)
These rules apply to every empty state, no exceptions.

Rule 1: One Primary Action Only
No forks. No choices.
Empty state = one obvious next step.

Rule 2: No Education Paragraphs
No “Here’s how it works”.
No “AI explanation”.
No marketing fluff.

Rule 3: No Promises
Never imply:

“Best”

“Optimized”

“AI decides”

“Results guaranteed”

Rule 4: Action > Explanation
Buttons beat text.
Text only clarifies why the button exists.

No mention of batch

Primary action
✅ Add Item

Explicitly NOT shown
❌ Import hints
❌ Batch generation CTA
❌ Tutorials

NOT allowed

Empty state ≠ education state.

Prevent feature creep (analytics, previews, suggestions)

Keep authority with the user

Keep mental load low

Make later UI changes additive, not corrective

LOADING & TRANSITIONAL STATES — SYSTEM SPEC
Why this layer matters
Loading states define:

Whether users trust the system

Whether they wait or panic

Whether they retry unnecessarily

Whether they think something is automatic

Bad loading UX causes:

Double clicks

Refreshes

Support tickets

Requests for “status dashboards”

We avoid all of that here.

GLOBAL LOADING RULES (NON-NEGOTIABLE)
Rule 1: Every loading state answers ONE question
“Is this working, and do I need to do anything?”

Never more.

Rule 2: Loading ≠ Thinking
Never imply intelligence, analysis, or decision-making.

Allowed:

“Generating image…”

“Processing items…”

Forbidden:

“Optimizing…”

“Choosing best…”

“AI is deciding…”

Rule 3: Disable > Warn
When something is in progress:

Disable conflicting actions

Do not show warnings or confirmations

Rule 4: Time honesty
If it might take time:

Say so plainly

Never give fake precision

Allowed:

“This may take a few minutes”

Forbidden:

“Almost done” (unless it actually is)

User opens app / project.

UI
Skeleton rows (not spinner)

No text needed

[▭ ▭ ▭]
[▭ ▭ ▭]

Skeleton must match real layout

Keeps user calm without gamifying progress.

[▭▭▭▭]

UI

Simple, honest.

Context

Pattern

[ Retry ]

❌ Silent failure

COPY SYSTEM — GLOBAL LANGUAGE RULES (LOCKED)
What the copy system controls
Button labels

Status messages

Confirmations

Errors

Success states

Irreversible actions

If copy is loose, UX discipline collapses.
So this is as strict as an API contract.

CORE PRINCIPLES (NON-NEGOTIABLE)
Principle 1: Action-First Language
Copy must describe what happens, not what the system is.

✅ “Generate image”
❌ “Let AI create an image”

Principle 2: No Authority Claims
The system never:

Decides

Chooses

Optimizes

Recommends outcomes

✅ “Select an image”
❌ “Best image selected”

Principle 3: Neutral, Not Friendly
No personality.
No cheerleading.
No anthropomorphism.

✅ Calm
❌ Cute
❌ Smart-sounding

Principle 4: Verbs Over Adjectives
Actions > descriptions.

✅ “Apply image”
❌ “Beautiful image ready”

VERB DICTIONARY (ALLOWED ONLY)
These are the only verbs allowed in UI unless explicitly approved.

Primary Action Verbs
Add

Create

Generate

Edit

Apply

Discard

Retry

Cancel

Save

Upload

Remove

Continue

Back

Secondary / Status Verbs
Processing

Generating

Applying

Saving

Loading

Fetching

Failed

Completed

🚫 Anything outside this list requires a conscious decision.

BUTTON COPY — EXACT RULES
Generation
Context Button
Single image Generate Image
Regenerate Generate Again
Batch start Start Batch
Retry failed Retry Failed Items
❌ “Generate with AI”
❌ “Create magic”

Apply / Commit Actions
Context Button
Save image Apply Image
Batch apply Apply Selected Images
Confirm edit Apply Changes
Never:

“Confirm”

“Finalize”

“Optimize”

Destructive Actions
Context Button
Remove image Remove Image
Discard draft Discard Draft
Cancel batch Cancel Batch
No euphemisms.
Destructive = explicit.

CONFIRMATION COPY (CRITICAL)
Confirmations exist only for irreversible actions.

Correct Pattern
Discard draft image?

This image will not be saved.

[ Discard ]
[ Cancel ]
Rules
Title = action

Body = consequence

Buttons = explicit

🚫 Never say:

“Are you sure?”

“This action cannot be undone” (too generic)

SUCCESS STATES (LOW-ENERGY BY DESIGN)
Success is acknowledgment, not celebration.

Examples
Image applied
Batch completed
Changes saved
No:

Checkmarks with praise

“You’re all set!”

Confetti

ERROR COPY SYSTEM
Structure (always 3 parts)
What failed

What user can do

One action

Example — Generation Failure
Image generation failed

You can try again.

[ Retry ]
Example — Batch Failure
Some items failed to generate

Retry failed items or review results.
❌ No error codes
❌ No stack traces
❌ No apologies

COST & USAGE COPY
Allowed
This batch will generate 24 images.
Usage will be recorded once generation starts.
Forbidden
❌ “This may cost credits”
❌ “Optimized usage”
❌ “Best value”

HISTORY & AUDIT COPY
History is factual, not narrative.

Examples
“Image generated”

“Image applied”

“Batch started”

“Batch cancelled”

“Image discarded”

❌ “User decided”
❌ “System chose”

TOOLTIP COPY (RARE, STRICT)
Tooltips are allowed only for:

Constraints

Disabled states

Example
This item is part of an active batch.
No:

Advice

Suggestions

Tips

FORBIDDEN WORDS (GLOBAL BAN)
These words must never appear anywhere:

AI

Smart

Intelligent

Best

Optimal

Recommended (except presets label)

Learns

Improves

Decides

Chooses

Automatically (unless literal)

ACCESSIBILITY COPY SYSTEM (LOCKED)
What this system governs
Accessibility copy controls:

Screen reader output

ARIA labels

Focus order narration

GLOBAL ACCESSIBILITY RULES
Rule 1: ARIA ≠ UI Copy
ARIA labels must be functional, not promotional.

✅ “Generate image for item”
❌ “Create a stunning image using AI”

ARIA explains what happens, never why it’s good.

Rule 2: Never Duplicate Visible Copy
If a button already has visible text:

ARIA-label must add context, not repeat

Example:

Button text: Generate Image

ARIA-label: Generate image for this item

Rule 3: State Changes Must Be Announced
Any async state change must be announced once — no loops.

Applies to:

Generation start

Generation completion

Batch completion

Errors

BUTTONS — ACCESSIBILITY COPY
Primary Buttons
Visible Label aria-label
Generate Image Generate image for this item
Generate Again Generate image again for this item
Apply Image Apply selected image to this item
Start Batch Start batch image generation
Cancel Batch Cancel batch image generation
🚫 Never include:

“AI”

“Recommended”

“Best”

ICON-ONLY BUTTONS (MANDATORY LABELS)
Icon-only buttons must always have aria-labels.

Icon aria-label
✕ Close dialog
🗑 Remove image
⟳ Retry generation
ⓘ More information
No exceptions.

IMAGE PREVIEWS — ALT TEXT RULES
Generated images must have neutral, factual alt text.

Pattern
Generated image of [item name]
Examples:

“Generated image of Margherita Pizza”

“Generated image of Iced Latte”

❌ Do NOT include:

Style adjectives (“beautiful”, “professional”)

Judgments

Marketing language

LOADING & STATUS ANNOUNCEMENTS
Single Image Generation
When generation starts:

aria-live: polite
"Image generation started"
When completed:

aria-live: polite
"Image generation completed"
When failed:

aria-live: assertive
"Image generation failed"
Batch Generation
Start:

"Batch generation started"
Progress (ONLY when meaningful change occurs):

"12 of 50 images generated"
Completion:

"Batch generation completed"
❌ Do NOT announce every single item completion
❌ Do NOT announce percentages continuously

ERRORS — SCREEN READER COPY
Errors must be:

Specific

Actionable

Short

Pattern
[What failed]. [What user can do].
Example:

Image generation failed. You can retry.
ARIA role:

role="alert"
CONFIRMATION MODALS — FOCUS & COPY
Focus rules
Focus moves to modal title

Focus trapped inside modal

Focus returns to triggering element on close

ARIA labeling

<div role="dialog" aria-labelledby="dialog-title">
Title text must be the action, not a question.

✅ “Discard draft image”
❌ “Are you sure?”

HISTORY & ACTIVITY — ACCESSIBILITY COPY
History items must be readable as events, not sentences.

Examples:

“Image generated”

“Image applied”

“Batch cancelled”

ARIA list role:

role="list"
role="listitem"
DISABLED STATES — REQUIRED EXPLANATION
If a control is disabled, screen readers must know why.

Pattern
aria-disabled="true"
aria-describedby="reason-id"
Reason text example:

This item is part of an active batch.
FORBIDDEN IN ACCESSIBILITY COPY (STRICT)
These words are banned even if UI copy changes later:

AI

Smart

Intelligent

Best

Recommended

Optimal

This is Localization Readiness, not translation.
We’re locking the system so this product can ship globally without rewriting UX later.

LOCALIZATION READINESS SYSTEM (LOCKED)
What this system governs
Localization readiness controls:

How strings are written

How variables are embedded

Whether grammar breaks in other languages

Whether RTL (right-to-left) layouts survive

Whether future translations force UI redesigns

If this is sloppy, localization becomes:

Expensive

Buggy

UX-breaking

CORE LOCALIZATION PRINCIPLES
Principle 1: Every String Must Stand Alone
No sentence should rely on surrounding UI context to make sense.

✅ “Batch completed”
❌ “Completed” (completed what?)

Principle 2: Never Concatenate Strings
No building sentences from fragments.

❌ "Batch " + status
❌ "Generated " + count + " images"

✅ Full sentence with variables:

"{count} images generated"
Why:
Grammar order changes across languages.

Principle 3: Variables Are Neutral Objects
Variables must never imply grammar, tense, or plurality.

Good:

"{count} images generated"
Bad:

"Generated {count} images"
Some languages don’t use past tense the same way.

VARIABLE & PLACEHOLDER RULES
Allowed placeholders
{count}

{itemName}

{batchName}

{date}

{time}

Never embed logic in placeholders.

❌ {count > 1 ? "images" : "image"}
❌ {itemName}s

Pluralization must be handled by the localization system, not UI code.

PLURALIZATION RULES (CRITICAL)
Every string involving counts must support plural forms, even if English doesn’t show them clearly.

Correct pattern
images_generated:
one: "{count} image generated"
other: "{count} images generated"
Never assume:

Only singular/plural

English plural rules

Some languages have 3–5 plural forms.

DATE, TIME & NUMBER NEUTRALITY
Dates
Never hardcode formats.

❌ Jan 28, 2026
❌ 28/01/2026

✅ Use abstract tokens:

"{date}"
Rendered via locale-aware formatter.

Time
Never say:

“AM / PM”

“Morning / Evening”

Use:

"{time}"
Numbers
No commas or decimals in strings.

❌ “1,000 images”
❌ “0.5 credits”

✅

"{count} images"
"{amount}"
Formatting happens at render-time.

DIRECTIONALITY (LTR / RTL SAFETY)
Rule 1: No directional words in copy
Avoid:

Left / Right

Start / End (unless abstract)

Prefer:

Primary / Secondary

First / Last

Rule 2: Icons Must Not Carry Meaning Alone
For RTL languages:

Arrows flip

Chevron direction flips

Meaning must come from text, not arrow direction.

BUTTON COPY — LOCALIZATION SAFE
Buttons must be:

Short

Verb-first

Context-independent

Good:

Generate Image

Apply Image

Start Batch

Retry Failed Items

Bad:

Try Again Now

Let’s Go

Continue Anyway

CONFIRMATION & ERROR COPY — TRANSLATION SAFE
Confirmation titles
Must be nouns or verb phrases, not questions.

✅ “Discard draft image”
❌ “Are you sure you want to discard this?”

Questions translate poorly and inflate length.

Error messages
No idioms.
No metaphors.

❌ “Something went wrong”
❌ “We hit a snag”

✅

"Image generation failed"
EMPTY & LOADING STATES — LOCALIZATION RULES
Empty states
Must be 2 lines max:

State

Action

Avoid descriptive paragraphs.

Loading states
Avoid tense-heavy language.

✅ “Generating image…”
❌ “We are generating your image…”

STRING LENGTH TOLERANCE (DESIGN RULE)
Design must tolerate:

30–50% longer strings than English

2-line button labels without breaking layout

If a button breaks when translated → the copy is wrong.

FORBIDDEN IN LOCALIZABLE COPY
These do not translate cleanly and must never be used:

AI magic

Smart

Effortlessly

Seamlessly

Instantly (unless measurable)

Perfect

SYSTEM CHECK — WHAT THIS ENABLES
By locking localization readiness now:

You can add languages without UX debt

RTL layouts won’t break logic

Copy remains calm and neutral worldwide

Legal and cultural risk is minimized

You never redesign buttons for translation

This is how real products go global without pain.

You now have five locked UX systems:

Screens & User Journey

That is a complete, production-grade UX specification.

🚨 WHAT IS THE NEXT BIG THING FOR PRODUCTION READINESS?
Short answer:

Operational Readiness + Failure Discipline

Most products die after good UX, not before.

You already have:

Features ✔️

UX systems ✔️

Copy discipline ✔️

Accessibility & localization ✔️

What you don’t yet fully lock is how the product behaves when:

Things go wrong

Users behave unexpectedly

Load increases

Money is involved

Support is needed at scale

That’s the real “production” cliff.

THE 4 REMAINING PRODUCTION-CRITICAL PILLARS
If this were a real readiness review, these are the only four I’d care about next.

1️⃣ FAILURE MODES & RECOVERY SYSTEM (MOST IMPORTANT)
Production readiness = how gracefully you fail.

You must explicitly define:
What happens when generation fails

What happens when batch partially fails

What happens when billing succeeds but generation fails

What happens when network drops mid-apply

What happens when user refreshes during critical actions

You’ve covered UX responses, but not system-level guarantees.

What’s missing right now
Canonical failure taxonomy (retryable vs terminal)

Idempotency rules

Safe re-entry guarantees

“No silent corruption” contract

👉 This is the #1 next thing.

2️⃣ DATA INTEGRITY & IDENTITY GUARANTEES
Screens don’t matter if data integrity breaks.

You need to lock:

What is the single source of truth

What can be duplicated

What can never be duplicated

What can be rolled back

What is append-only forever (audit)

Examples:

Can an image be applied twice?

Can two batches touch the same item?

Can a retry create duplicates?

Can history be mutated?

Right now, these are implicitly assumed, not explicitly frozen.

3️⃣ BILLING, USAGE & DISPUTE SAFETY
Billing is not pricing — it’s trust infrastructure.

You must define:

When usage is recorded (exact moment)

What happens on partial success

What happens on retries

How users dispute usage

What support can and cannot reverse

If this is fuzzy, you’ll bleed support hours and refunds.

This is not a UI problem, it’s a policy + logic problem.

4️⃣ SUPPORT & OPERATIONAL PLAYBOOK (SOLO-FOUNDER CRITICAL)
Since you’re solo, this one matters more for you than for teams.

You need:

A support taxonomy (what tickets exist)

A triage priority system

A “what support is allowed to do” rulebook

A debugging surface (internal, not UI)

Clear escalation paths (even if it’s just you)

Without this, every issue becomes a panic.

WHAT IS NOT THE NEXT BIG THING (IMPORTANT)
Just to be explicit — do not jump to:

❌ More features
❌ Performance optimizations
❌ Marketing experiments
❌ Analytics dashboards
❌ AI improvements

All of these are post-production optimizations.

RECOMMENDED NEXT SEQUENCE (EXECUTION ORDER)
If I were running this, I’d do it in this exact order:

STEP 1 — Failure & Recovery Spec (Hard)
Failure types

Retry rules

Idempotency

Resume guarantees

STEP 2 — Data & State Contracts
Immutable vs mutable

Ownership rules

Locking guarantees

STEP 3 — Billing & Usage Ledger Rules
When charged

When refunded

What is disputable

STEP 4 — Support & Ops Playbook
Ticket categories

Internal debugging checklist

Resolution authority

Only after this do you ship confidently.

MY STRONG RECOMMENDATION
Say one of these and we’ll go deep, same rigor as UX:

“Start with Failure & Recovery System” (best next step)

“Data integrity contracts”

“Billing & dispute logic”

“Support & ops playbook”

If you skip this layer, the product will look production-ready but won’t survive production.

1️⃣ FAILURE MODES & RECOVERY SYSTEM (HIGHEST PRIORITY)
1.1 Failure Taxonomy (Must Be Exhaustive)
Define and classify every possible failure:

Single image generation failure

Image editing failure

Batch item failure

Partial batch failure

Full batch failure

Timeout failure

Network interruption

API quota exceeded

Cloud task retry exhaustion

Storage upload failure

DB write failure

Permission / auth failure

User-initiated cancel vs system abort

1.2 Failure Type Classification
For each failure above, define:

Retryable vs non-retryable

User-visible vs silent

Immediate vs delayed

Item-scoped vs batch-scoped

System fault vs user fault

1.3 Retry Rules (Hard Rules)
Lock rules for:

Max retries per item

Retry backoff strategy

Manual retry vs automatic retry

Partial retry vs full retry

Retry after refresh

Retry after cancel

Retry after billing event

1.4 Idempotency Guarantees
Explicitly define:

What operations are idempotent

Idempotency keys per action

Duplicate request handling

Safe replays after refresh

Cloud task duplicate execution handling

1.5 Resume & Re-entry Guarantees
Define behavior when user:

Refreshes mid-generation

Closes tab mid-apply

Switches device

Loses network temporarily

Logs out mid-process

1.6 “No Silent Corruption” Rules
Guarantees such as:

Images never auto-apply

Failed states are always visible

History is append-only

Drafts cannot overwrite applied images

Partial writes are recoverable

2️⃣ DATA INTEGRITY & STATE CONTRACTS
2.1 Canonical Source of Truth
Define clearly:

What is the authoritative item record

What is derived

What is cached

What is immutable

What can be regenerated safely

2.2 State Machine Definitions
Explicit state machines for:

Item image state

Draft image state

Batch job state

Batch item state

Editing session state

(Valid transitions only — no ad-hoc jumps)

2.3 Concurrency & Locking Rules
Define:

Item-level locks

Batch-level locks

Cross-batch conflicts

Manual override precedence

Safe unlock conditions

2.4 Duplication & Uniqueness Rules
Lock down:

Can the same image exist twice?

Can the same prompt generate multiple stored images?

How duplicates are detected (or not)

What is allowed to repeat vs forbidden

2.5 Rollback & Cleanup Guarantees
Define:

What gets cleaned on cancel

What persists after failure

When storage objects are deleted

When history remains forever

Orphaned resource handling

3️⃣ BILLING, USAGE & DISPUTE LOGIC
3.1 Usage Event Definition (Atomic)
Define exactly:

When usage is counted

At request start vs success vs completion

Single vs multi-image charge

Batch accounting granularity

Editing charges vs generation charges

3.2 Partial Success Billing Rules
Explicit policies for:

Batch partially successful

Retry after partial success

Failure after billing

Cancel mid-batch

User-initiated vs system failure

3.3 Ledger Model
Define:

Append-only ledger rules

Adjustment entries

Refund entries

Audit visibility

Immutable vs reversible records

3.4 Dispute Handling Rules
Lock:

What users can dispute

Time window for disputes

What support can reverse

What cannot be reversed

How disputes affect trust limits

3.5 Abuse & Cost Protection

Hard usage caps

4.3 Support Authority Boundaries

Can support edit history?

Image exists but system fails after.

Storage upload failure

Partial upload

Storage permission denied

Metadata write failure

DB write timeout

Transaction logging failure

History append failure

B. IMAGE EDITING FAILURES
Editing is riskier than generation because it mutates.

B1. Edit Input Failures
Original image missing

Original image deleted mid-session

Unsupported edit feature

Invalid edit prompt

Reference images missing

Multiple reference conflict

B2. Edit Processing Failures
Model rejects edit

Model times out

Partial edit response

Output image invalid

Output image identical to input (no-op)

B3. Edit Application Failures
Edited image fails quality guard

Storage overwrite failure

New image stored but not linked

Old image accidentally removed

History not updated

C. BATCH GENERATION FAILURES (MOST COMPLEX)
Batch is multiple independent failures pretending to be one.

C1. Batch Setup Failures
No eligible items

Items already in another batch

Item list changed mid-setup

Batch size exceeds limit

Terms not accepted

Invalid config inherited by all items

C2. Batch Trigger Failures
Batch job doc not created

Cloud Tasks enqueue failure

Partial task enqueue

Duplicate task enqueue

Job status stuck in queued

C3. Batch Item-Level Failures
For each item independently:

Item deleted mid-batch

Item edited mid-batch

AI generation failure (A2/A3)

Storage failure

Retry exhaustion

Task execution timeout

Duplicate task execution

C4. Batch Aggregation Failures
Generated count mismatch

Status never reaches terminal state

Some tasks finish after cancel

Job marked completed with failures

Job marked failed despite partial success

C5. Batch Cancel Failures
Cancel request lost

Tasks still running after cancel

Tasks complete after cancel

Partial results exist after cancel

Cancelled job reappears on refresh

D. USER & SESSION FAILURES
These are guaranteed to happen in real life.

Browser refresh mid-generation

Tab closed mid-apply

Network drop mid-upload

Session expires mid-batch

User logs in from another device

Same user opens same item in two tabs

E. BILLING-ADJACENT FAILURES (NO LOGIC YET)
We are not solving billing yet, just identifying touchpoints.

Usage logged but generation fails

Generation succeeds but logging fails

Partial batch success before failure

Retry causes double charge risk

Cancel after billing event

Dispute after retry

F. SYSTEM & INFRA FAILURES
These are rare but catastrophic.

Firestore latency spikes

Cloud Tasks delayed hours

Storage quota exceeded

Logger unavailable

Clock skew affecting timestamps

Partial deploy during active batch

G. HUMAN-ERROR FAILURES (SUPPORT & OPS)
You must assume you will make mistakes.

Manual DB edit mistake

Manual storage cleanup mistake

Refund issued incorrectly

Job retriggered manually

History entry removed accidentally

2️⃣ DATA INTEGRITY & STATE — MASTER BREAKDOWN
This area has 4 sub-domains. We will take them one by one.

2A. Core Entities & Ownership

STEP 2A — CORE ENTITIES & OWNERSHIP
Goal:
Define what exists, who owns it, and what it is allowed to reference.

If ownership is unclear, everything downstream breaks.

2A.1 ENTITY INVENTORY (CANONICAL)
These are the only first-class entities that matter for integrity:

User

Project

Item

Image (Generated or Uploaded)

Batch Job

Generation Attempt

Usage Ledger Entry

Audit Event

If anything is not on this list → it is derived or ephemeral.

2A.2 OWNERSHIP GRAPH (NON-NEGOTIABLE)
We lock a strict parent → child graph.

User
└── Project
└── Item
├── Image
├── Generation Attempt
└── Audit Event
└── Batch Job
├── Generation Attempt(s)
└── Audit Event
Rules:
No entity has two parents

No cross-project references

No orphan entities

If parent is deleted → children become inaccessible (even if retained).

2A.3 ENTITY OWNERSHIP RULES (CRITICAL)
User
Owns: Projects

Never owns: Images directly

Project
Owns: Items, Batch Jobs

Boundary for billing & quotas

Item
Owns: Images, Attempts

Cannot reference images from another item

Image
Belongs to exactly one Item

Never shared

Never global

Batch Job
Belongs to exactly one Project

References multiple Items (read-only)

Does NOT own images directly

Generation Attempt
Belongs to one Item OR one Batch Job

Immutable once created

Usage Ledger Entry
Belongs to exactly one Attempt

Append-only

Audit Event
Belongs to exactly one entity

Never edited

Never deleted

2A.4 HARD INTEGRITY GUARANTEES
These are system invariants:

❌ An image cannot exist without an item

❌ An image cannot be reassigned

❌ A batch job cannot apply images automatically

❌ Attempts cannot be merged

❌ Ledger entries cannot be reversed (only compensated)

Violating any of these is a P0 production bug.

2A.5 FORBIDDEN RELATIONSHIPS (EXPLICIT)
These must never exist:

❌ Image → Project (direct)

❌ Image → Batch Job

❌ Item → Item

❌ Batch Job → Batch Job

❌ User → Image

If you ever feel tempted → architecture is wrong.

STEP 2B — MUTABILITY RULES
(What can change, when it can change, and who is allowed to change it)

This section prevents silent corruption, time-travel bugs, and “how did this change?” incidents.

No UX. No implementation. Just laws.

2B.1 MUTABILITY CATEGORIES (FOUNDATION)
Every entity or field must fall into one category only:

Immutable — never changes after creation

Append-only — can grow, never shrink or overwrite

Mutable (Controlled) — can change, but only under strict conditions

If something doesn’t clearly fit → it’s a design flaw.

2B.2 ENTITY-BY-ENTITY MUTABILITY MATRIX
User
Immutable: id, createdOn

Mutable: name, email, authProviders

Append-only: loginEvents

Rule: User identity is stable; metadata may evolve.

Project
Immutable: id, ownerId, createdOn

Mutable: name, settings, status

Append-only: auditEvents

Rule: Ownership never changes. Projects may be renamed or disabled, never reassigned.

Item
Immutable: id, createdOn

Mutable: name, description, attributes

Append-only: images[], generationAttempts[], auditEvents[]

Hard rule:
❌ An image reference is never removed, only deprecated (soft-hidden).

Image
Immutable: id, source, createdOn, attemptId

Mutable: ❌ NONE

Append-only: ❌ NONE

This is critical:
An image is a historical artifact, not a living object.

If you allow mutation here → you break trust and history.

Batch Job
Immutable: id, projectId, createdOn, itemSet

Mutable: status (restricted lifecycle only)

Append-only: statusHistory[], attempts[], auditEvents[]

Rule:
Batch jobs evolve only via state transitions, never field edits.

Generation Attempt
Immutable: ALL fields

Mutable: ❌ NONE

Append-only: ❌ NONE

Reason:
Attempts are facts, not processes. Facts never change.

Usage Ledger Entry
Immutable: ALL fields

Mutable: ❌ NONE

Append-only: ❌ NONE

Rule:
Corrections happen via new ledger entries, never edits.

Audit Event
Immutable: ALL fields

Mutable: ❌ NONE

Append-only: ❌ NONE

2B.3 STATUS TRANSITION RULES (VERY IMPORTANT)
For entities with status, only forward transitions are allowed.

Batch Job (example)
queued → processing → completed → finished
↘ failed
↘ cancelled
Rules:

❌ No backward transitions

❌ No skipping required states

❌ No manual overrides outside system rules

If state is wrong → new corrective state, not overwrite.

2B.4 WHO IS ALLOWED TO MUTATE?
User
Can mutate: Item metadata, selection decisions

Cannot mutate: Attempts, Images, Ledger, Audit

System
Can mutate: Status fields (within lifecycle)

Can append: Attempts, Images, Ledger entries

Support
❌ Cannot mutate production data

✅ Can append audit notes

✅ Can trigger compensating actions

If support can “edit data”, you’ve already lost control.

2B.5 FORBIDDEN MUTATIONS (ABSOLUTE)
These must never happen:

❌ Editing an image after creation

❌ Editing an attempt

❌ Deleting ledger entries

❌ Reassigning ownership

❌ Rewriting history to “fix” bugs

Violations here = P0 incident.

2B.6 WHY THIS MATTERS (REALITY CHECK)
If you lock these rules:

Debugging becomes deterministic

Billing disputes are resolvable

Trust is preserved

Support stays sane

You can scale safely

If you don’t:

Every bug becomes a mystery

Every refund becomes manual

Every audit becomes impossible

STEP 2C — CONCURRENCY & LOCKING
(How the system behaves when things happen at the same time — which they will)

If this is vague, duplicate images, double charges, and corrupt state are guaranteed.

No UX. No retries. Only rules.

2C.1 CONCURRENCY SURFACES (WHERE COLLISIONS CAN HAPPEN)
First, we explicitly name every place concurrency exists:

Same Item opened in multiple tabs

Same Item included in two batch jobs

Batch job running while user edits item

User triggers retry while task is still running

Cloud Task retries overlapping with manual retry

User applies image while batch still generating

Support action during live job

Session expires + resumes elsewhere

If a surface is not listed → you’re blind to it.

2C.2 LOCKING PHILOSOPHY (GLOBAL)
This system uses soft, scoped locks, not hard DB locks.

Core principles:
Locks are narrow (item-level, not project-level)

Locks are time-bound

Locks never block reads

Locks block actions, not UI

Locks fail predictably

Hard locks = deadlocks + support nightmares.

2C.3 ITEM-LEVEL LOCKS (MOST IMPORTANT)
When an Item is considered “locked”
An Item enters a locked state when:

A generation attempt is in-flight

The item is part of an active batch

An image apply operation is in progress

What is blocked during item lock
❌ Start another generation
❌ Include item in another batch
❌ Edit item attributes that affect generation
❌ Apply a different image

What is allowed during item lock
✅ View item
✅ View existing images
✅ Cancel the active operation
✅ Navigate away

Rule:

Only one generation-affecting action per item at a time.

2C.4 BATCH-LEVEL LOCKS
Batch jobs do not lock the project.

They only assert read-locks on items.

Rules:
An item can belong to only one active batch

Batch reads item snapshot at start

Later item edits do not affect running batch

Batch never mutates items directly

If batch mutated items → disaster.

2C.5 ATTEMPT-LEVEL IDEMPOTENCY LOCK
Every Generation Attempt has a unique idempotency key.

Guarantees:
Same attempt cannot execute twice

Retries reference same attempt

Duplicate Cloud Task execution is ignored

Billing attaches to attempt, not request

This is what prevents:

Double image creation

Double billing

Phantom retries

2C.6 APPLY-IMAGE CRITICAL SECTION
Applying an image is a critical operation.

Rules:

Apply is atomic: either linked or not

Apply is idempotent

Apply checks item lock

Apply cannot run concurrently with generation

If apply is not atomic → corruption.

2C.7 MULTI-TAB & MULTI-SESSION BEHAVIOR
Explicitly allowed:

Multiple tabs open

Same item viewed in parallel

Explicitly blocked:

Parallel mutations

Parallel generation triggers

System must:

Detect stale state

Reject unsafe actions

Never “guess” intent

2C.8 SUPPORT & MANUAL ACTIONS
Support actions do not bypass locks.

Rules:

Support can cancel jobs

Support cannot force-apply images

Support cannot unlock items manually

Support actions create audit events

If support can bypass locks → integrity is gone.

2C.9 FORBIDDEN CONCURRENCY (ABSOLUTE)
These must never occur:

❌ Two attempts generating for same item

❌ Two batches touching same item

❌ Apply during active generation

❌ Retry creating new attempt

❌ Concurrent apply operations

Any of these = P0 incident.

WHY THIS MATTERS (REALITY)
This section alone:

Prevents duplicates

Prevents double billing

Prevents corrupted UI states

Prevents “how did this happen?” tickets

Most SaaS products skip this and pay forever.
