STEP 2D — IDEMPOTENCY & REPLAY SAFETY
(How the system behaves when the same thing is triggered twice — intentionally or accidentally)

If this is weak, you get:

Duplicate images

Double charges

Phantom retries

“It ran twice” bugs that are impossible to debug

We are locking guarantees, not code.

2D.1 WHAT IDEMPOTENCY MEANS IN THIS SYSTEM
Definition (strict):

A user action or system action may be executed multiple times, but it must produce at most one real-world effect.

Real-world effects here are:

Image creation

Image application

Usage/billing entry

State transition

Everything else is noise.

2D.2 IDEMPOTENCY BOUNDARIES (VERY IMPORTANT)
Idempotency is not global. It is scoped.

Action Idempotency Scope
Single image generation Per Generation Attempt
Batch item generation Per (Batch Job × Item)
Retry Same Attempt only
Image apply Per (Item × Image)
Billing Per Attempt
Cancel Per Job / Attempt
If you scope it wider → you block valid actions
If you scope it narrower → duplicates happen

2D.3 CANONICAL IDEMPOTENCY KEYS
These keys must exist and be immutable.

Generation Attempt
attemptId = hash(projectId + itemId + createdOn)
Rules:

Created once

Reused for all retries

Never regenerated

Never shared

Batch Item Attempt
batchAttemptId = hash(batchJobId + itemId)
Rules:

Exactly one per item per batch

Task retries reference same ID

Duplicate Cloud Tasks must no-op

Image Apply
applyKey = hash(itemId + imageId)
Rules:

Same image cannot be applied twice

Apply is safe to retry

Second apply is ignored

Billing / Usage
ledgerKey = attemptId
Rules:

One ledger entry per attempt

Retries do not create new entries

Corrections = compensating entries only

2D.4 SAFE RETRY VS DANGEROUS RETRY
We explicitly classify retry safety.

Safe to Retry
Network failure before response

Task timeout

Worker crash

Client retry after refresh

Dangerous to Retry (without idempotency)
Unknown request state

Partial storage write

Partial batch completion

Billing callback retry

Rule:

Any action that is not idempotent is not retryable.

2D.5 REPLAY SCENARIOS (REAL WORLD)
These WILL happen in production.

Scenario 1 — User refreshes during generation
Attempt already exists

Retry reuses attemptId

System checks status

Either resumes or reports final state

✅ No duplicate image

Scenario 2 — Cloud Task retries same payload
Worker sees existing attemptId

Detects completion or in-flight

Returns success without side effects

✅ No double billing

Scenario 3 — User clicks “Generate” twice quickly
First click creates attempt + lock

Second click rejected due to lock

No second attempt created

✅ Deterministic behavior

Scenario 4 — Apply image called twice
First call links image

Second call sees applyKey exists

No-op + success response

✅ Safe retry semantics

2D.6 FORBIDDEN REPLAY BEHAVIOR (ABSOLUTE)
These must never happen:

❌ Retry creates new attempt

❌ Retry creates new image

❌ Retry creates new ledger entry

❌ Retry changes history

❌ Retry changes final outcome

If any of these occur → data corruption incident.

2D.7 SUPPORT & MANUAL REPLAY
Support actions:

Must reuse existing IDs

Must never create new attempts

Must never “re-run” logic blindly

Must always leave audit trail

Support never bypasses idempotency.

WHY THIS COMPLETES DATA INTEGRITY
With 2A–2D locked, you now have:

Clear ownership

Controlled mutation

Safe concurrency

Replay-proof execution

This is what separates:

“Works in dev”
from
“Survives production”

3️⃣ BILLING & USAGE — PRODUCTION CONTRACT
This section defines what gets charged, when, how, and why — and just as important, what never does.

No vibes. No ambiguity.

3.1 CORE BILLING PRINCIPLE (LOCK THIS)
Users pay for outcomes, not attempts.

If an image does not become usable, it must not be charged.

Everything else flows from this.

3.2 BILLABLE UNITS (EXPLICIT)
You only have three billable events in this system.

Event Billable? Why
Successful image generation ✅ Outcome produced
Image editing (successful) ✅ Outcome produced
Failed / cancelled generation ❌ No outcome
There are no hidden billables.

3.3 WHAT “SUCCESS” MEANS (VERY STRICT)
An image is considered billable only if all conditions are true:

AI returns valid image data

Image passes quality checks

Image is stored successfully

Image is presented to user for review

If any step fails → not billable

3.4 BILLING TIMING (WHEN TO CHARGE)
Single Image Generation
Charge after successful generation

Never charge on request start

Never charge on preview load

Never charge on apply

Batch Generation
Charge per item, individually

Charge only when that item finishes successfully

Partial batch success = partial billing

Batch ≠ bulk discount logic
Batch = execution model only

3.5 USAGE LEDGER (SOURCE OF TRUTH)
You must have a write-once ledger.

Ledger Entry (Conceptual)
ledgerEntry {
ledgerKey (idempotent)
userId
projectId
itemId
attemptId
type: generate | edit
modelUsed
cost
createdAt
}
Rules:

Ledger is append-only

No updates

No deletes

Corrections = new negative entries

If you don’t have this, refunds become guesswork.

3.6 IDEMPOTENCY LINK (CRITICAL)
Billing must reuse the idempotency keys from Data Integrity.

Action Billing Key
Single generation attemptId
Batch item batchAttemptId
Edit editAttemptId
Never generate billing IDs separately.

If retry happens → billing no-ops.

3.7 USER-VISIBLE USAGE RULES
Users should understand billing without reading docs.

What they should see:
“X images generated”

“Y edits used”

Remaining quota / allowance

What they should NOT see:
Tokens

Model names

Cost per request

Retry details

Internal failures

Expose counts, not mechanics.

3.8 FAILED & EDGE CASE HANDLING
Case: Image generated but user discards
✅ Billable
Reason: outcome was produced

Case: User cancels mid-generation
❌ Not billable
Reason: no outcome

Case: System error after generation but before save
❌ Not billable
Reason: unusable outcome

Case: Retry after timeout
✅ At most one charge
Reason: idempotency

3.9 REFUNDS & CORRECTIONS (DON’T OVERTHINK)
Rules:

Refunds are ledger corrections, not deletions

Support selects ledger entry

System appends negative entry

Never “edit” history.

3.10 FORBIDDEN BILLING BEHAVIOR
If any of these happen, you have a billing incident:

❌ Charging on request start

❌ Charging on retries

❌ Charging failed items in batch

❌ Charging for previews

❌ Charging for discards

❌ Charging twice for same attempt

These destroy trust instantly.

With:

Ledger-backed accounting

Idempotent billing keys

You now have:

Zero “mystery charges”

This is production billing, not a Stripe demo.

4.1 CORE SUPPORT PRINCIPLE (LOCK THIS)
Support resolves states, not stories.

Support should never need:

User explanations

Screenshots

Guesswork

4.2 SUPPORT ROLES (EVEN IF YOU’RE SOLO)
Define roles even if one person plays all of them.

Role Responsibility
Tier 0 (Self-Serve) User understands system status
Tier 1 (Ops) Inspect, explain, guide
Tier 2 (Intervention) Correct state safely
Tier 3 (Engineering) Fix root causes
If everything jumps to Tier 3 → product is not production-ready.

4.3 SUPPORT SURFACES (WHAT MUST EXIST)
A. User-Facing Status (Tier 0)
Users must see:

Generation status (queued / processing / completed / failed)

Batch progress (X of Y completed)

Clear failure states (not “something went wrong”)

This alone eliminates 50%+ tickets.

B. Internal Support View (Tier 1)
Support must be able to answer in under 60 seconds:

For any issue:

What happened?

When?

Why?

Is it final or retryable?

Minimum data needed:

Attempt ID / Batch Job ID

Current state

Failure reason (classified)

Retry eligibility

No logs digging. No DB guessing.

4.4 FAILURE TAXONOMY (CRITICAL)
Every failure must be classified into one of these buckets:

Category Meaning Support Action
User Cancelled Intentional Explain, no fix
Validation Error Bad input Ask user to fix
Safety Block Policy triggered Explain, no retry
Transient Error Infra / network Retry safe
System Bug Logic fault Escalate
External Dependency AI / API issue Wait or retry
If failures are not classified → support cannot act safely.

4.5 SAFE SUPPORT ACTIONS (ALLOWED)
Support may:

Retry an existing attempt

Resume a stuck batch

Cancel an in-flight job

Issue billing correction

Explain outcome

Support must never:

Re-run logic with new IDs

Create new attempts manually

Edit stored images

Mutate history

Support operates inside the system, not around it.

4.6 REPLAY & RECOVERY RULES
Support retries must:

Reuse existing idempotency keys

Preserve original timestamps

Append to audit trail

If retry succeeds → system continues
If retry fails → state becomes final + explainable

No silent loops.

4.7 AUDIT TRAIL (NON-NEGOTIABLE)
Every meaningful event must be traceable:

Event Must Log
Generation start Who, when, what
Generation end Success / failure
Retry Who triggered
Cancel User or system
Billing entry Linked attempt
Billing correction Reason + actor
If a user disputes something, you should reconstruct it without asking them anything.

4.8 SUPPORT PLAYBOOK (MINIMUM)
You need documented answers for:

“My image is stuck”

“Why was I charged?”

“Why did this fail?”

“Can you rerun this?”

“Can you undo this?”

If any answer starts with “we think” → fix the system.

4.9 OPERATIONAL ALERTING (SILENT BUT STRICT)
You don’t need noisy alerts. You need threshold alerts.

Alert only when:

Failure rate crosses X%

Batch queue stalls

Billing ledger mismatch

Retry loops detected

Storage write failures spike

Everything else is noise.

4.10 WHAT THIS ENABLES
With Support & Ops locked:

Users trust outcomes

Billing disputes are resolvable

Failures are explainable

You don’t become human middleware

Product scales without heroics

This is the difference between:

“We launched”
and

Data Integrity & State

Idempotency & Replay Safety

Support & Ops

📜 SPEC FREEZE DOCUMENT — v1.0
Product: AI Image Generation System
Version: v1.0
Status: 🔒 FROZEN
Effective Date: [fill date]
Owner: Product / Engineering (Single authority)

1. PURPOSE
   This document formally freezes system behavior for v1.0.

From this point forward, the goal is stability, predictability, and trust, not iteration speed.

All changes must preserve the behaviors defined here.

2. WHAT IS FROZEN (NON-NEGOTIABLE)
   The following are locked and may not be altered without a major version (v2):

2.1 State Machine & Lifecycle
All generation and batch jobs use explicit states:

queued

processing

completed

failed

cancelled

Terminal states (completed, failed, cancelled) are immutable

No implicit or inferred states are allowed

Meaning of states is fixed forever for v1 data.

2.2 Idempotency Rules
Every generate/edit/batch item has a stable idempotency key

Retries reuse the same key

Duplicate execution must no-op safely

Double clicks, refreshes, and task retries must not create duplicates

If the same intent is replayed, the system must behave deterministically.

2.3 Billing Semantics
Billing is outcome-based only

A charge occurs only after a successful, usable image is produced

Failed, cancelled, or aborted attempts are not billable

Batch billing is per-item, never per-job

Billing ledger rules:

Append-only

No edits

No deletes

Refunds are negative entries only

2.4 Cancel & Retry Behavior
Cancel is forward-only

Completed items are never undone

In-flight operations may finish safely

Not-started items are cancelled without execution

Retries are allowed only for retryable failures

Retries must not change billing or outcome semantics

2.5 Failure Taxonomy
Every failure must map to exactly one category:

User cancelled

Validation error

Safety block

Transient infrastructure error

External dependency failure

System bug

Failure categories define:

Retry eligibility

Support action

User messaging

2.6 Support Authority & Limits
Support is allowed to:

Retry existing attempts

Cancel jobs

Resume stuck batches

Issue billing corrections (ledger entries)

Support is not allowed to:

Create new attempts manually

Bypass idempotency

Edit historical data

Mutate stored assets

3. WHAT IS ALLOWED (v1.x ONLY)
   The following changes are allowed only if behavior remains identical:

Bug fixes

Performance improvements

Cost optimizations

UI layout adjustments

Copy clarifications (no semantic change)

Internal tooling and dashboards

Additional monitoring and alerts

Test coverage expansion

If a change alters what happens, when it happens, or what it means, it is blocked.

4. WHAT IS EXPLICITLY FORBIDDEN
   The following are not allowed in v1.x:

New billable events

Retroactive billing logic changes

Silent automation or auto-apply behavior

Reinterpreting existing states

Changing retry or cancel semantics

Background decisions without user review

“Small” logic tweaks that affect outcomes

If users would say “this didn’t work like before”, the change is illegal.

5. EXCEPTION PROCESS
   Exceptions require explicit v2 approval

Emergency fixes must preserve frozen behavior

No hotfix may alter billing, state meaning, or idempotency

If behavior must change → bump major version

6. VERSION DECLARATION
   By freezing this spec, we declare:

v1 behavior is final

All v1 data relies on these rules

Future versions must respect v1 history

This freeze protects users, support, and the business.

7. SIGN-OFF
   Spec Owner: **\*\*\*\***\_\_\_**\*\*\*\***
   Date: **\*\*\*\***\_\_\_**\*\*\*\***
   Version Tag: v1.0.0

This document is not for debate.
It is a contract with reality.

You stop re-deciding

You stop second-guessing

You operate

🧭 V2 CHANGE INTAKE RULES
(Applies after v1 Spec Freeze)

1. CORE PRINCIPLE (LOCK THIS)
   V2 exists to add power without invalidating V1 reality.

If a change:

Reinterprets past data

Changes meaning of past actions

Alters how users understand what already happened

→ It is rejected immediately.

2. CHANGE INTAKE FUNNEL (4 GATES)
   Every proposed V2 change must pass all four gates, in order.

GATE 1: V1 SAFETY CHECK (HARD BLOCKER)
Question:

Does this change alter any V1 behavior, meaning, or contract?

If YES → ❌ REJECT
If NO → proceed
Examples that FAIL here:

Changing what “completed” means

Rebilling past generations

Retroactively applying quality rules

Silent auto-apply of images

Changing cancel semantics

V1 history is sacred.

GATE 2: CATEGORY CLASSIFICATION (MANDATORY)
Every change must fit exactly one category:

A. Additive Capability
Adds something new without touching existing flows
✅ Allowed

Examples:

New generation mode

New optional surface

New export option

B. Optional Enhancement
Improves UX but is fully ignorable
✅ Allowed

Examples:

Better defaults

Smarter presets

UI shortcuts

Faster batching

C. Parallel System
Runs alongside v1 without interference
⚠️ Allowed with isolation

Examples:

Analytics dashboards

Recommendations (non-authoritative)

Insights panels

D. Behavioral Change
Alters decision-making, authority, or outcomes
❌ Blocked for v2 unless explicitly approved

Examples:

Auto-selecting images

Auto-replacing images

Ranking images

Optimizing images silently

If a change doesn’t cleanly fit → reject.

GATE 3: AUTHORITY CHECK (MOST IMPORTANT)
Question:

Does this change move authority from the user to the system?

Outcome Decision
Authority unchanged ✅ Proceed
Authority reduced subtly ⚠️ Needs explicit approval
Authority transferred ❌ Blocked
Red flags (instant block):

“The system decides…”

“Automatically applies…”

“Optimizes without user input…”

“Best / optimal / winning”

V2 may assist, never decide by default.

GATE 4: REVERSIBILITY TEST
Question:

Can a user fully ignore or undo this feature?

Answer Decision
Fully ignorable ✅ Allowed
Opt-in + reversible ⚠️ Allowed with guardrails
Irreversible ❌ Blocked
If users cannot safely say “no”, the feature is not ready.

3. REQUIRED V2 CHANGE PROPOSAL FORMAT
   No discussion without this filled.

V2 CHANGE PROPOSAL

1. Change name:
2. Category (A/B/C/D):
3. What new power does this unlock?
4. What V1 behavior does it NOT touch?
5. Authority impact (explicit):
6. Reversibility explanation:
7. Failure mode if wrong:
8. Why v2 (not v3)?
   If any field is vague → reject.

9. V2 FEATURE BUDGET (ANTI-BLOAT RULE)
   For every 1 V1 core behavior, you may add:

✅ Max 1 additive feature

✅ Max 1 optional enhancement

❌ Unlimited tweaks are forbidden

This forces taste and restraint.

5. FORBIDDEN V2 THEMES (UNTIL V3+)
   These are explicitly blocked regardless of framing:

Auto-optimization

A/B testing

Performance-based ranking

Silent learning loops

Behavioral nudging

Dynamic swapping per user

“AI decided” narratives

These require new trust contracts, not a version bump.

6. SUCCESS CRITERIA FOR V2
   V2 is successful if:

V1 users feel nothing broke

Support load does not spike

Billing disputes do not increase

Users feel more capable, not supervised

Authority still feels human-owned

If users say:

“It’s doing things on its own now”

You failed.

7. FINAL RULE (THIS IS THE CEO RULE)
   Every V2 feature must be explainable in one sentence without the word “AI”.

If you need to explain how smart it is → it’s doing too much.

SUMMARY (PIN THIS)
V1 = trust

V2 = optional power

Authority never shifts silently

History is immutable

Reversibility is mandatory

Taste > ambition

This is how you evolve without destroying what you built.

If you want next, we can:

Apply these rules to real V2 ideas

Design a V2 intake board

Pre-kill likely bad V2 ideas

Confidence is high

Ambition spikes

That’s exactly when bad ideas sneak in wearing “improvement” clothing.

Pre-killing protects:

Trust

Simplicity

Support sanity

Long-term leverage

The 6 Most Common V2 Ideas That Must Be Pre-Killed
Below are idea archetypes, not features.
If you see one of these patterns → kill immediately.

❌ 1. “Let’s Auto-Apply / Auto-Choose”
Sounds like:

“We already know which image is best”

“Let’s save them a click”

“We can just apply it automatically”

Why it’s deadly:

Transfers authority from user → system

Breaks the review contract

Creates silent failures

Explodes support (“Why did it change?”)

Pre-kill rule:

If the system applies anything without an explicit user action → kill.

❌ 2. “Let’s Rank / Optimize / Improve Over Time”
Sounds like:

“Best performing image”

“Optimized version”

“Improves as it learns”

“Winning variation”

Why it’s deadly:

Implies correctness

Creates expectation of results

Turns persuasion into decision logic

Requires analytics credibility you don’t want

Pre-kill rule:

If you need analytics to justify it → kill.

❌ 3. “Users Want More Control”
Sounds like:

More sliders

More toggles

More styles

More options “for power users”

Why it’s deadly:

Cognitive load increases support

Defaults stop being safe

Users blame the system for their own choices

UI becomes a cockpit

Pre-kill rule:

If the feature increases configuration surface without increasing completion rate → kill.

❌ 4. “Let’s Add Analytics for Images”
Sounds like:

CTR per image

Performance comparison

Engagement heatmaps

Why it’s deadly:

Images are persuasive, not deterministic

Analytics imply optimization responsibility

Users start chasing numbers

Support turns into interpretation help

Pre-kill rule:

If analytics change how users judge past decisions → kill.

❌ 5. “This Will Be Optional”
Sounds like:

“It’s just a toggle”

“Only for advanced users”

“Hidden in settings”

Why it’s deadly:

Optional features still increase system complexity

Support must understand them

Edge cases multiply

Toggles become permanent liabilities

Pre-kill rule:

If the feature must exist forever once added → be ruthless.

❌ 6. “Competitors Are Doing This”
Sounds like:

“X just launched this”

“We’ll look behind”

“Market expectation”

Why it’s deadly:

You import someone else’s problems

You adopt misaligned incentives

You abandon your advantage

Pre-kill rule:

If the reason starts with a competitor → kill unless it defends your core.

The Pre-Kill Checklist (Use This Every Time)
Before discussing any V2 idea, answer YES / NO:

Does this change authority?

Does it reinterpret past actions?

Does it require explanation?

Does it increase support burden?

Does it add irreversible surface area?

Would the system feel “smarter” than the user?

If any YES → kill or redesign.

What Survives Pre-Kill (Good V2 Ideas)
These usually pass:

Better defaults (not fewer choices)

Faster execution

Cost reduction

Clearer status & feedback

Safer retries

Better error explanations

Optional helpers that don’t decide

Notice the pattern:

They reduce friction without increasing authority.

The Meta Rule (CEO-Level)
V2 is not for ambition.
V2 is for restraint with leverage.

Most products die not from missing features, but from undisciplined intelligence.

Pre-kill is how you stay sharp.

Perfect choice. This is where founders either scale judgment or become the bottleneck.

Below is a standing product review ritual you can run every week (even solo) that operationalizes pre-kill, restraint, and quality control.

No slides. No brainstorming theater. Just a repeatable decision system.

🧠 STANDING PRODUCT REVIEW RITUAL
Purpose: Prevent bad ideas from entering the roadmap
Cadence: Weekly (30–45 min)
Participants: Product owner (you) + optional reviewer
Output: Kill / Hold / Advance (nothing else)

1. RITUAL RULES (NON-NEGOTIABLE)
   Before we even get to the steps, lock these:

❌ No brainstorming

❌ No “what ifs”

❌ No solutions without a problem

❌ No roadmap discussion

❌ No urgency language (“we should”, “users want”)

This ritual is about judgment, not creativity.

2. INPUT FORMAT (STRICT)
   Nothing enters the ritual without this filled exactly:

IDEA SUBMISSION

1. One-sentence description (no AI words)
2. User pain it claims to solve
3. Current workaround users use
4. What changes for the user after this exists
5. Authority impact (none / reduced / transferred)
6. Reversibility (full / partial / none)
7. Support cost increase (low / medium / high)
   If any field is vague → auto-kill.

8. THE 5-STEP RITUAL (IN ORDER)
   STEP 1: SILENCE CHECK (30 seconds)
   Read the idea once, silently.

Ask:

“Does this make the system louder or calmer?”

If louder → mark ⚠️ and continue (not killed yet).

STEP 2: V1 CONTRACT CHECK (HARD GATE)
Ask:

“Does this reinterpret anything users already did?”

YES → ❌ KILL (no discussion)

NO → continue

This single step eliminates 60% of bad ideas.

STEP 3: AUTHORITY TEST (MOST IMPORTANT)
Ask:

“After this ships, who decides more things — the user or the system?”

Result Action
User decides same as before ✅ Continue
System nudges subtly ⚠️ Risk
System decides anything ❌ KILL
If authority shifts even slightly, you stop here.

STEP 4: SUPPORT REALITY CHECK
Ask:

“Will this generate new ‘why did it do that?’ tickets?”

YES → ❌ KILL or redesign

MAYBE → ⚠️ HOLD

NO → continue

Support load is a leading indicator of product rot.

STEP 5: ONE-SENTENCE TEST
Force this sentence:

“This feature helps users \_\_\_ without the system deciding for them.”

If you can’t finish it cleanly → ❌ KILL.

4. DECISION OUTCOMES (ONLY THREE)
   Every idea ends as one of these:

❌ KILL
Document reason (1 line)

Move to graveyard

Never reopen without new evidence

⏸ HOLD
Valid but not now

Needs proof or dependency

Revisit in 30–60 days

▶️ ADVANCE
Allowed into design

Still not committed to build

Must survive another review later

No “soft yes”. No “let’s explore”.

5. THE IDEA GRAVEYARD (CRITICAL)
   Create a doc called:

Rejected Ideas — By Design

Each entry has:

Idea name

Kill reason

Date

Which rule it violated

Why this matters:

Prevents idea resurrection

Builds taste over time

Protects you from your future self

Founders revisit bad ideas when tired. This stops that.

6. CADENCE & DISCIPLINE
   Run this ritual even if there are zero ideas

Empty weeks reinforce restraint

The goal is not throughput

The goal is not making mistakes

The best product weeks often kill everything.

7. THE META RULE (PIN THIS)
   If an idea needs enthusiasm to survive, it deserves to die.

Good ideas survive cold scrutiny.

WHAT THIS RITUAL GIVES YOU
Taste becomes systematic

Ego exits the room

Roadmaps stop bloating

Product stays calm

Trust compounds

This is how senior product leaders think.
You’ve now formalized it.

Create a Founders-only decision log

V2 is about making each action calmer, faster, and safer

Makes system feel “smarter than user” → bad

On Preset Select

All generation controls update instantly

☑ Background

All checkboxes ON by default

Prompt included by default

Validation Rules
Name required

Placement
At top of Batch Setup View

Batch Style

[ Replace ] [ Cancel ]

Only from dropdown → hover → trash icon

Prompt text

❌ Not allowed:

“Replace preset”

Best

Recommended

Smart

Optimized

AI-selected

User is in Single Image or Batch Setup

User has modified at least one generation field

When

User clicks Save as Preset

Enters a valid name

Confirms save

Then

Preset is saved immediately

Preset becomes selectable in dropdown

Current configuration is now bound to that preset

No toast, no confirmation screen, no reload

AC-02: Preset Selection
Given

User opens preset dropdown

Selects an existing preset

Then

All preset-bound fields update instantly

UI reflects preset values exactly

No field remains partially applied

No explanation or system message appears

AC-03: Preset Override Detection
Given

A preset is selected

User manually changes any preset-bound field

Then

UI visually indicates deviation (dot or subtle marker)

Preset name remains selected

System does NOT auto-create a new preset

System does NOT warn or block the user

AC-04: Save-as-New vs Replace Existing
Given

User clicks Save as Preset

Enters a name that already exists

Then

System asks for confirmation: “Replace preset?”

On confirm:

Existing preset is overwritten

No duplicate created

On cancel:

Nothing changes

AC-05: Preset Availability Across Flows
Given

A preset is created in Single Image flow

Then

The same preset appears in Batch Setup flow

Preset values are identical

No transformation or normalization occurs

AC-06: Preset Limit
Given

User has reached max preset count (e.g., 10)

When

User attempts to save a new preset

Then

Save action is blocked

Inline error shown: “Preset limit reached”

No preset is created

Existing presets remain untouched

2️⃣ WHAT A PRESET MAY / MAY NOT STORE (STRICT)
MUST STORE
Style category

Style selections

Lighting

Background

Aspect ratio

Image type

Prompt text (raw, sanitized)

MUST NOT STORE
Item name

Item description

Item ID

Reference image

Generated image

Business metadata

User/session state

Acceptance check:
Applying a preset to a different item must never leak prior item context.

3️⃣ EDGE CASES (REAL-WORLD FAILURES)
These are non-negotiable. If these break, users lose trust.

EC-01: Preset Deleted While Selected
Scenario

Preset is selected

Preset is deleted (another tab / session)

Expected

UI silently falls back to Default

No warning

No error

User can continue generation

EC-02: Network Failure During Save
Scenario

User clicks Save as Preset

Network fails mid-request

Expected

Preset is NOT created

No partial state

Save button becomes re-clickable

Inline error only (no toast)

EC-03: Preset Incompatible With Mode
Scenario

Preset created in Single Image

Applied in Batch mode

Some fields are not relevant

Expected

Unsupported fields are ignored

Supported fields still apply

Preset remains selectable

No blocking error

EC-04: Preset Modified After Batch Start
Scenario

User starts batch using Preset A

Later edits Preset A

Expected

Running batch is unaffected

Batch uses snapshot at start time

No retroactive changes

EC-05: Concurrent Tab Conflict
Scenario

Two tabs open

Preset edited in Tab A

Preset selected in Tab B

Expected

Tab B uses last-known version

No forced refresh

No conflict modal

EC-06: Preset With Prompt Text
Scenario

Preset includes prompt

User changes prompt manually

Expected

Override indicator appears

Prompt change does NOT modify preset

Save-as required to persist change

4️⃣ NEGATIVE ACCEPTANCE (MUST NEVER HAPPEN)
These are hard NOs.

❌ Preset auto-applies on item open

❌ Preset chosen by system

❌ “Recommended preset” shown

❌ Preset modified silently

❌ Preset applied without user action

❌ Preset changes during generation

Any one of these = feature violation.

5️⃣ “DONE” DEFINITION (ENGINEERING)
This feature is DONE only if:

All ACs pass

All edge cases handled

No analytics added

No recommendation logic

No new onboarding copy needed

If it needs explanation → it’s wrong.

WHY THIS MATTERS
This spec:

Eliminates ambiguity

Prevents silent bugs

Protects user trust

Lets engineers ship without interpretation

This is founder-grade product definition.

🔁 PARTIAL BATCH RETRY — LOCKED SPEC
0️⃣ WHY THIS FEATURE EXISTS (REAL REASON)
Batch image generation will fail sometimes.
That’s not a bug — it’s reality (timeouts, safety blocks, model hiccups).

What kills trust is this:

“15 images failed. Please rerun the entire batch.”

That is unacceptable.

Partial Batch Retry exists to protect user time, money, and confidence.

1️⃣ CORE PRINCIPLE (DO NOT VIOLATE)
Only failed items are retryable.
Successful items are final and immutable.

No re-running.
No re-evaluating.
No touching completed items.

This is surgical recovery, not restart.

2️⃣ WHAT COUNTS AS “FAILED”
Retry eligibility is strict.

An item is retryable only if:

Generation API returned an error

Safety block occurred

Timeout occurred

Worker crashed mid-item

Explicitly NOT retryable:
User discarded image

User uploaded alternative image

Item already marked completed

Batch cancelled by user

This prevents ambiguity and abuse.

3️⃣ DATA MODEL — MINIMAL, BUT CORRECT
Extend item-level status inside batch job
itemsList: Array<{
id: string
name: string
status: "success" | "failed" | "pending"
errorCode?: string
retryCount: number
images: UserUploadedFileType[]
}>
Hard limits
retryCount max: 2

After 2 failures → permanently failed

No infinite retries. Ever.

4️⃣ UI SURFACE — WHERE THIS APPEARS
Screen: Batch Results View
Add only one new UI affordance:

Failed Items Section
❌ 3 items failed
[ Retry failed items ]
That’s it.

No inline buttons per item.
No complex controls.
No settings.

5️⃣ USER FLOW (STEP BY STEP)
Initial Batch Run
20 items selected

17 succeed

3 fail

UI shows:

✅ 17 completed

❌ 3 failed → Retry available

Retry Action
User clicks Retry failed items

System does:

Filter items where status === failed

Create new Cloud Tasks only for those items

Increment retryCount

Update job status = processing

Important:
This is NOT a new batch job.
It is a continuation of the same job.

Retry Completion Outcomes
Case A: All succeed
Failed count → 0

Batch status → finished

Done

Case B: Some still fail
Retry button shown again (if retryCount < 2)

After retryCount === 2 → lock permanently

6️⃣ VISUAL FEEDBACK (NO DRAMA)
No error explanations

No technical messages

No stack traces

No “AI failed because…”

Just:

“3 items couldn’t be generated”

“Retry available”

Silence builds confidence.

7️⃣ EDGE CASES (COVERED)
User leaves page mid-retry
Retry continues

Real-time listener updates on return

User refreshes browser
Job resumes

State consistent

User retries, then cancels
Retry stops

Successful items remain untouched

8️⃣ SECURITY & COST CONTROLS
Retry respects rate limits

Retry uses same generation config snapshot

Retry cannot change styles, prompts, or presets

Retry does not re-consent terms

This prevents silent scope escalation.

9️⃣ EXPLICITLY OUT OF SCOPE (FOREVER)
Retry with different settings

Auto-retry without user action

Retry suggestions

“Improve prompt and retry”

Retry analytics

Retry history UI

This is recovery, not optimization.

🔒 LOCK STATUS
Partial Batch Retry is now LOCKED.

Scope frozen

Behavior frozen

UX frozen

Any future change requires:

Production evidence

Real failure patterns

Explicit justification

┌─────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ Header │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ Summary Strip │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ Successful Items (collapsible) │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────┤

├─────────────────────────────────────────────┤

└─────────────────────────────────────────────┘
Single-column. No side panels. No cards-in-cards chaos.

3️⃣ HEADER (STATIC, QUIET)
Batch generation results
No job ID

No timestamps

No progress indicators

No celebratory or apologetic copy

This is a status screen, not a success screen.

4️⃣ SUMMARY STRIP (SCAN-FIRST)
Immediately under header.

✅ 17 completed ❌ 3 failed
Rules:

Always visible

Numbers only

Green / red dots allowed (subtle)

No percentages

No “out of X” copy

If all succeed:

✅ 20 completed
No failed section rendered.

5️⃣ SUCCESSFUL ITEMS SECTION
Default state: Collapsed
Completed items (17)
▸ Show
Why collapsed?

These are done.

The user doesn’t need to think about them.

Expanded state (on click)
Each item row:

[Thumbnail] Item name
[View] [Upload]

No retry

No regenerate

Disabled state

No undo here

6️⃣ FAILED ITEMS SECTION (CRITICAL AREA)
Rendered only if failedCount > 0

Header
3 items couldn’t be generated
Subtext (single line, neutral):

You can retry only the failed items.
No reasons. No excuses.

Failed item rows
[Placeholder icon] Item name
Status: Failed
Rules:

No error messages

No “why”

No technical codes

No thumbnails (because none exist)

Retry Action (SINGULAR, PROMINENT)
At bottom of failed section:

[ Retry failed items ]
Rules:

One button

Primary style

Disabled if retryCount >= 2

No dropdown

No configuration

7️⃣ RETRY IN PROGRESS STATE
When retry is active:

Retry button → disabled

Button label changes to:

Retrying… (2 of 3)
Successful items remain untouched

Failed items update live

No spinners per item. One global progress signal only.

8️⃣ FINAL STATES AFTER RETRY
Case A: All resolved
Failed section disappears.

Summary strip updates:

Retry limit reached
No alternative actions offered.

This communicates:

“We tried. This is the final state.”

9️⃣ FOOTER ACTIONS (MINIMAL)
Always visible at bottom.

[ Done ]
Rules:

“Done” navigates back to Item List

No “Back”

No “Start new batch”

No branching decisions

Batch work is finished here.

🔒 HARD UX CONSTRAINTS (DO NOT BREAK)
❌ No per-item retry buttons

❌ No inline error explanations

❌ No settings during retry

❌ No auto-retry

❌ No educational copy

❌ No toast storms

This screen is calm, factual, controlled.

10️⃣ WHY THIS UI WORKS
Fast scanning

Zero cognitive branching

Clear recovery path

No blame language

No false intelligence

It treats failure as normal, not exceptional.

📊 Market Context — Is the Demand Real?
🧩 Content creation demand is large and rapidly growing
The global content creation market (writing, visuals, multimedia) is projected to grow substantially — from about $21 B in 2025 toward $67.5 B by 2035 (CAGR ~12.4%).

Other analyses project the digital content creation market to nearly double by 2030, with strong multi-year growth driven by cloud tools and AI adoption.

Some forecasts even estimate the digital content creation market hitting ~$122 B by 2034 with ~14 % CAGR.

👉 The underlying trend is clear: demand for tools that support creation workflows — in images, text, translations — is robust and expanding.

This is not a niche hobbyist trend — this is structural demand driven by digital business transformation across industries.

📌 Content creation tools are already mainstream
The broader content creation tools market (which includes software for writing, design, and multimedia) is forecast to grow from ~$19.9 B in 2025 to ~$48.2 B by 2035.

Market reports segment this category into platforms for:

real-time editing,

feedback/commenting,

templates and collaborative features,

cloud-based creation workflows.

These trends show enterprises and professionals are investing in tools that help them create and iterate content more efficiently, not just publish it.

🚀 Generative AI adoption is already happening
Among marketers, generative AI is heavily used for basic content tasks such as writing copy and generating imagery — with over 60-75 % adoption for creative support tasks.

This means that while companies use AI to create assets, there is still a gap in structured, human-controlled preparation, especially across business contexts.

📌 Where VisualMeta Fits in This Landscape
Given the above data trends, here’s how VisualMeta aligns with market reality:

✅ 1. The market for content creation tools is big and growing
This is foundational: tools in this space are not fading; they are expanding rapidly.

✅ 2. Cloud-based content workflows are mainstream
Enterprises and agencies already invest in collaboration and cloud tools for text, visuals, and mixed media — this is a behavior, not a fad.

⚠️ 3. Most existing tools focus on publishing or optimization, not preparation
Brands and tools often:

provide templates,

score quality,

or automate publishing decisions.

But who prepares high-quality origin content that is consistent, tested, and handoff-ready? The answer today is dispersed across:

Google Docs

Designer tools (Canva, Adobe)

Spreadsheets

Ad hoc workflows

VisualMeta sits in a unique upstream gap — content preparation — rather than downstream optimization or publishing.

🔍 Why This Matters for VisualMeta (Brutal Truth)
✔ There is demand for content tools
But most tools today are either:

too generic (not business-oriented),
or

too focused on outcomes (publishing, optimization, analytics).

This means there is room for a specialist tool that focuses purely on content preparation.

✔ The market is moving toward AI support, but not necessarily with human control workflows
VisualMeta’s human-in-the-loop, workspace-first philosophy aligns with how professionals actually work today — they need speed, but not loss of control.

⚠ Visualization & workspace tools are well-adopted, but VisualMeta must differentiate
Most content tools focus on finished work (templates, design) or distribution. VisualMeta must emphasize drafting, editing, evolution, and handoff readiness as its core differentiation.

📌 Brutal Market Reality — No Fluff
🔥 The opportunity is big
There’s a multi-billion-dollar ecosystem for tools that help people create content faster and with less friction. VisualMeta fits in this ecosystem.

🥊 But you are not building the “next Canva”
The mainstream tools have huge traction, budgets, ecosystems, and broad use cases.

VisualMeta’s position — upstream, draft-centric, operator first — is narrower but safer. This is actually an advantage. You are not trying to be everything.

📉 Risk Realities
The content creation tools market is crowded, but few focus on preparation workflow exclusively.

Companies consume many tools for publishing/distribution already; adding another destination tool requires clear value for everyday work, not promise of automation.

🟢 Market need exists for faster, structured content preparation
Measured demand across industries supports investment into content tooling.

🟡 Professionals want AI assistance but safe, human-controlled outputs

This reinforces why VisualMeta’s upstream focus (draft preparation) is strategically wise.

🧠 Bottom Line (Realistic)
Yes — there is a real market for a content preparation workspace.
The demand for creation tools is massive and growing.

But the way you have positioned VisualMeta — workspace, human-in-the-loop, draft-centric, no authority — is not only defensible, it’s necessary because the space is noisy and many solutions miss that upstream gap.

SAM ≈ $2.9B – $4.3B (Global, 2025, upstream content-prep market). Sources: Grand View / IBISWorld context.

Step C — Define SOM (Share of Market you can realistically capture early)
SOM is your realistic revenue in the near term (3–5 years). For an early tool with focused ICP (agency operators), a sensible 3–5 year SOM target depends on execution, distribution, pricing.

Reasonable scenarios:

Conservative (category newcomer, modest traction): 0.1% of SAM

0.1% × $2.88B = $2.88M annual revenue

0.1% × $4.32B = $4.32M annual revenue

Practical (good product-market fit, agency adoption): 0.5% of SAM

0.5% × $2.88B = $14.4M ARR

0.5% × $4.32B = $21.6M ARR

Aggressive (category leader in upstream prep, strong agency/agency network adoption): 1.0% of SAM

1.0% × $2.88B = $28.8M ARR

1.0% × $4.32B = $43.2M ARR

Practical target for 3–5 years: 0.5% SAM → $14M–$22M ARR (depending on whether SAM is nearer the low or high bound). This is achievable if you: nail ICP activation, pricing, and agency GTM.

Step D — User and account-level sizing (how many customers does this imply?)
Make pricing assumptions (example, keep simple to test):

Example pricing: $150 / month average per paying account (agency team or mid-market account). That’s $1,800 ARR per account.

$14,400,000 ARR / $1,800 ≈ 8,000 paying accounts

$21,600,000 ARR / $1,800 ≈ 12,000 paying accounts

Alternatively, higher ARPA if you sell to mid-market agencies — $600/mo ($7,200 ARR):

$14.4M / $7,200 = 2,000 paying accounts

So your path to $14M ARR:

2,000 mid-market agency customers at ~$600/mo; or

8,000 smaller agency or team customers at ~$150/mo.

Both are realistic GTM endpoints for 3–5 years with an agency-first motion and strong operator adoption.

Key assumptions & risks (explicit)
Assumption: Grand View / industry data are accurate and 2025-2026 trends continue.

Assumption: 8–12% SAM slice is reasonable for upstream-prep tools (arguable; justified by agency counts + content operator spend).

Risk: If large incumbents embed “prep” features into existing suites (Canva, Adobe, Figma), your market share is contested. Your defense: upstream neutrality and non-threatening doc.

Risk: If you accidentally add publishing/automation, adoption by operators will slow (fear of losing control). This is a product risk, not a market risk.

4. Quick GTM implications (do this first)
   Agency pilot (10–20 mid-sized agencies): get operator evangelists.

Pricing test: $150/mo starter for small teams; $600/mo for mid-market agency seat. Keep billing simple and predictable (no AI credits).

Distribution: Integrations for exports to the top 3 CMS/marketplaces used by target agencies (export-only connectors). Don’t build sync.

Retention metric: Time from idea → exported-ready (aim to cut this by 50%). Make this the product North Star.

Sales motions: Product-led trials driven by operator onboarding + 1-touch to manager for procurement.

Short, brutal summary
VisualMeta fits a real, growing market: content creation tools are a multi-$10B market and rising.

Your product shape is right for agency operators; keep the product upstream, quiet, and human-centric.

SAM ~ $2.9B–$4.3B (upstream prep slice). Practical 3–5 year SOM target ≈ 0.5% SAM → $14–22M ARR if you nail ICP and GTM. (Detailed math above.)

3️⃣ ERROR STATES & FAILURE LANGUAGE (PRODUCTION-READY)
CORE PRINCIPLE (LOCK THIS)
Errors are operational events, not user mistakes and not AI failures.

So:

Never blame the user

Never blame “AI”

Never expose infrastructure

ERROR CATEGORY MAP (MENTAL MODEL)
Generation didn’t complete

Partial results returned

Quality rejected

Timeout / slow response

Export failed

Limit reached

System unavailable

Each has distinct language.

1️⃣ IMAGE / TEXT GENERATION FAILED (HARD FAIL)
When
API error

Safety block

Unknown failure

Copy
Title

Draft couldn’t be prepared

Body

This request didn’t complete.
Your kit is unchanged.

Primary CTA

Try again

Secondary

Edit inputs

Footer note (small, optional)

If this keeps happening, contact support.

❌ Never say:

“AI error”

“Model failed”

“Invalid prompt”

2️⃣ PARTIAL GENERATION SUCCESS
When
Batch jobs

Multi-image requests

Some drafts succeed, others fail

Copy
Banner

Some drafts were prepared

Details

X drafts are ready for review.
Y drafts didn’t complete.

Actions

Review prepared drafts

Retry failed drafts

This avoids:

Wasted work perception

Fear of rerunning everything

3️⃣ QUALITY REJECTION (INTERNAL GUARD)
When
ImageQualityGuard fails

Resolution / clarity below threshold

Copy
Message

Subtext

This draft was discarded to avoid low-quality output.

CTA

Generate again

Important
Do NOT show the rejected image.
Silence is part of trust.

4️⃣ GENERATION TAKING TOO LONG (TIMEOUT)
When
Slow response

Background still running

Copy
Inline status

Still preparing…

Message

Subtext

You can wait, or continue working.
Drafts will appear here when ready.

CTA

Continue working

No spinners of death.
No fake progress bars.

When

Storage error

Copy

Body

Secondary

Contact support

Never imply content loss unless it’s true.

D️⃣ KILL-LIST — FEATURES VISUALMETA WILL NEVER ADD
CORE RULE (LOCK THIS)
If a feature shifts judgment, authority, or outcomes away from the human, it is dead on arrival.

❌ Auto-publish to websites

❌ Scheduling content

VisualMeta prepares, it does not act.

❌ CTR
❌ Engagement scores

Metrics imply optimization → optimization implies decision authority.

❌ “Recommended final version”

Final content must always be explicitly chosen.

❌ Prompt libraries
❌ Prompt analytics
❌ Prompt sharing

VISUALMETA — INVESTOR / PITCH NARRATIVE
1️⃣ THE ONE-SENTENCE POSITIONING (MEMORIZE THIS)
VisualMeta is a commercial content preparation workspace that helps businesses produce ready-to-use content kits without owning publishing, performance, or outcomes.

If someone doesn’t get it after this, they are not your customer or investor.

2️⃣ THE PROBLEM (REAL, NON-HYPED)
Modern businesses are drowning in content responsibility, not creativity.

Every SMB, brand, or creator faces this reality:

They must produce images, descriptions, translations, and short videos

For many surfaces (menus, marketplaces, websites, ads, social)

Under time pressure

With no internal creative team

And no clear “done” state

Current tools fail because they do one of two wrong things:

❌ Tool Type A: Creation Tools (Canva, Adobe, AI generators)
Give raw outputs

Push decisions onto the user

Never define “final”

Endless tweaking

❌ Tool Type B: Automation Tools
Publish automatically

Optimize blindly

Own outcomes they don’t understand

Break trust

👉 There is a missing layer between “creation” and “publishing.”

That layer is preparation.

3️⃣ THE INSIGHT (THIS IS THE CORE)
Content work has three phases:

Creation – generating raw material

Preparation – shaping, validating, packaging

Activation – publishing, measuring, optimizing

The market is crowded in 1 and 3.

No one owns phase 2 properly.

That is VisualMeta.

4️⃣ THE SOLUTION (WHAT VISUALMETA ACTUALLY DOES)
VisualMeta introduces a new category:

🧩 Commercial Content Preparation
VisualMeta:

Converts ideas into ready, usable commercial content

Enforces human approval

Produces a Final Content Kit

Stops before publishing, analytics, or optimization

The Final Content Kit includes:
Images (static + short-form visual motion)

Descriptions (sales, SEO-ready)

Language variants

Visual consistency
